E_{\alpha}
. If
E/C
is a fibred site, then
\mathcal{F}
is a sheaf with respect to the total topology on
E
if and only if
\mathcal{F}_{\alpha}
is a sheaf on
E_{\alpha}
for each
\alpha
(
[
SGA 4

II

, VI.7.4.7]
).
Moreover, we have the following description of a covanishing sheaf.

Proposition 6.6

(
[
AGT16
, VI.5.10]
)
.

Let
E/C
be a covanishing fibred site. Then, a presheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
E
is a sheaf if and only if the following conditions hold:

(v)

The presheaf
\mathcal{F}_{\alpha}=\iota_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{p}}\mathcal{F}
on
E_{\alpha}
is a sheaf for any
\alpha\in\mathrm{Ob}(C)
.

(c)

For any covering family
\{f_{i}:\alpha_{i}\to\alpha\}_{i\in I}
of
C
, if we set
\alpha_{ij}=\alpha_{i}\times_{\alpha}\alpha_{j}
and
f_{ij}:\alpha_{ij}\to\alpha
, then the sequence of sheaves on
E_{\alpha}
,

(6.6.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\alpha}\to\prod_{i\in I}f_{i*}\mathcal{F}_{\alpha_{i}}\rightrightarrows\prod_{i,j\in I}f_{ij*}\mathcal{F}_{\alpha_{ij}},

is exact.

7.
Faltings Ringed Sites

7.1
.

Let
Y\to X
be a morphism of
\mathbb{U}
-small coherent schemes, and let
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}
be the category of morphisms
V\to U
of
\mathbb{U}
-small coherent schemes over the morphism
Y\to X
, namely, the category of commutative diagrams of coherent schemes

\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X}

Given a functor
I\to\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}
sending
i
to
V_{i}\to U_{i}
, if
\lim V_{i}
and
\lim U_{i}
are representable in the category of coherent schemes, then
\lim(V_{i}\to U_{i})
is representable by
\lim V_{i}\to\lim U_{i}
. We say that a morphism
(V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime})\to(V\to U)
of objects of
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}
is
Cartesian
if
V^{\prime}\to V\times_{U}U^{\prime}
is an isomorphism. It is clear that the Cartesian morphisms in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}
are stable under base change.

Consider the functor

(7.1.5)

\displaystyle\phi^{+}:\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{/X},\ (V\to U)\longmapsto U.

The fibre category over
U
is canonically equivalent to the category
\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{/U_{Y}}
of coherent
U_{Y}
-schemes, where
U_{Y}=Y\times_{X}U
. The base change by
U^{\prime}\to U
gives an inverse image functor
\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{/U_{Y}}\to\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{/U^{\prime}_{Y}}
, which endows
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}/\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{/X}
with a structure of fibred category. We define a presheaf on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}
by

(7.1.6)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathscr{B}}(V\to U)=\Gamma(U^{V},\mathcal{O}_{U^{V}}),

where
U^{V}
is the integral closure of
U
in
V
.

Definition 7.2
.

Let
Y\to X
be a morphism of coherent schemes. A morphism
(V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime})\to(V\to U)
in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}
is called
étale
, if
U^{\prime}\to U
is étale and
V^{\prime}\to V\times_{U}U^{\prime}
is finite étale.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
Y\to X
be a morphism of coherent schemes,
(V^{\prime\prime}\to U^{\prime\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\longrightarrow}}(V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\longrightarrow}}(V\to U)
morphisms in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}
.

(1)

If
f
is étale, then any base change of
f
is also étale.

(2)

If
f
and
g
are étale, then
f\circ g
is also étale.

(3)

If
f
and
f\circ g
are étale, then
g
is also étale.

Proof.

It follows directly from the definitions.
∎

7.4
.

Let
Y\to X
be a morphism of coherent schemes. We still denote by
X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
(resp.
X_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}
) the site formed by coherent étale (resp. finite étale)
X
-schemes endowed with the étale topology.
Let
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
be the full subcategory of
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}
formed by
V\to U
étale over the final object
Y\to X
. It is clear that
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is stable under finite limits in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}
. Then, the functor (
7.1.5
) induces a functor

(7.4.1)

\displaystyle\phi^{+}:\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\longrightarrow X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\ (V\to U)\longmapsto U,

which endows
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}/X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
with a structure of fibred sites, whose fibre over
U
is the finite étale site
U_{Y,\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}
. We endow
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
with the associated covanishing topology, that is, the topology generated by the following types of families of morphisms

(v)

\{(V_{m}\to U)\to(V\to U)\}_{m\in M}
, where
M
is a finite set and
\coprod_{m\in M}V_{m}\to V
is surjective;

(c)

\{(V\times_{U}{U_{n}}\to U_{n})\to(V\to U)\}_{n\in N}
, where
N
is a finite set and
\coprod_{n\in N}U_{n}\to U
is surjective.

It is clear that any object of
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is quasi-compact by
6.4
.
We still denote by
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
the restriction of the presheaf
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}
to
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
if there is no ambiguity.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
Y\to X
be a morphism of coherent schemes. Then, the presheaf on
\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{/Y}
sending
Y^{\prime}
to
\Gamma(X^{Y^{\prime}},\mathcal{O}_{X^{Y^{\prime}}})
is a sheaf with respect to the fpqc topology
(
[
Sta22
,
022A
]
)
.

Proof.

We may regard
\mathcal{O}_{X^{Y^{\prime}}}
as a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-algebra over
X
. It suffices to show that for a finite family of morphisms
\{Y_{i}\to Y\}_{i\in I}
with
Y^{\prime}=\coprod_{i\in I}Y_{i}
faithfully flat over
Y
, the sequence of quasi-coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-algebras

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{X^{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{X^{Y^{\prime}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{X^{Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}}}}

is exact. Thus, we may assume that
X=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)
is affine. We set
A_{0}=\Gamma(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y})
,
A_{1}=\Gamma(Y^{\prime},\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\prime}})
,
A_{2}=\Gamma(Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}Y^{\prime},\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}})
,
R_{0}=\Gamma(X^{Y},\mathcal{O}_{X^{Y}})
,
R_{1}=\Gamma(X^{Y^{\prime}},\mathcal{O}_{X^{Y^{\prime}}})
,
R_{2}=\Gamma(X^{Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}},\mathcal{O}_{X^{Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}}})
. Notice that
R_{i}
is the integral closure of
R
in
A_{i}
for
i=0,1,2
(
[
Sta22
,
035F
]
). Consider the diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{2}}

We see that the vertical arrows are injective and the second row is exact by faithfully flat descent. Notice that
R_{0}=A_{0}\cap R_{1}
, since they are both the integral closure of
R
in
A_{0}
as
A_{0}\subseteq A_{1}
. Thus, the first row is also exact, which completes the proof.
∎

Proposition 7.6
.

Let
Y\to X
be a morphism of coherent schemes. Then, the presheaf
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is a sheaf.

Proof.

It follows directly from
6.6
, whose first condition holds by
7.5
, and whose second condition holds by
3.17
(cf.
[
AGT16
, III.8.16]
).
∎

Definition 7.7

(
[
Fal02
, page 214]
,
[
AGT16
, VI.10.1]
)
.

We call
(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\overline{\mathscr{B}})
the
Faltings ringed site
of the morphism of coherent schemes
Y\to X
.

It is clear that the localization
(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})_{/(V\to U)}
of
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
at an object
V\to U
is canonically equivalent to the Faltings ringed site
\mathbf{E}_{V\to U}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
of the morphism
V\to U
by
6.4
(cf.
[
AGT16
, VI.10.14]
).

7.8
.

Let
Y\to X
be a morphism of coherent schemes. Consider the natural functors

(7.8.1)

\displaystyle\psi^{+}

\displaystyle:\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\longrightarrow Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\ (V\to U)\longmapsto V,

(7.8.2)

\displaystyle\beta^{+}

\displaystyle:Y_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}\longrightarrow\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\ V\longmapsto(V\to X),

(7.8.3)

\displaystyle\sigma^{+}

\displaystyle:X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\longrightarrow\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\ U\longmapsto(Y\times_{X}U\to U).

They are left exact and continuous (cf.
[
AGT16
, VI 10.6, 10.7]
). Then, we obtain a commutative diagram of sites associated functorially to the morphism
Y\to X
by
2.5
,

\textstyle{Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{Y_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}

where
\rho:Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to Y_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}
is defined by the inclusion functor, and the unlabelled arrow
Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is induced by the morphism
Y\to X
. Moreover, if
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
denotes the structural sheaf on
X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
sending
U
to
\Gamma(U,\mathcal{O}_{U})
, then
\sigma^{+}
actually defines a morphism of ringed sites

(7.8.8)

\displaystyle\sigma:(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\overline{\mathscr{B}})\longrightarrow(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}).

We will study more properties of these morphisms in the remaining sections.

Lemma 7.9
.

Let
X
be the spectrum of an absolutely integrally closed valuation ring,
Y
a quasi-compact open subscheme of
X
. Then, for any presheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
, we have
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{a}}(Y\to X)=\mathcal{F}(Y\to X)
. In particular, the associated topos of
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is local
(
[
SGA 4

II

, VI.8.4.6]
)
.

Proof.

Notice that
Y
is also the spectrum of an absolutely integrally closed valuation ring by
3.11
.(
1
) and that absolutely integrally closed valuation rings are strictly Henselian. Thus, any covering of
Y\to X
in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
can be refined by the identity covering by
6.4
. We see that
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{a}}(Y\to X)=\mathcal{F}(Y\to X)
for any presheaf
\mathcal{F}
. For the last assertion, it suffices to show that the section functor
\Gamma(Y\to X,-)
commutes with colimits of sheaves. For a colimit of sheaves
\mathcal{F}=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\mathcal{F}_{i}
, let
\mathcal{G}
be the colimit of presheaves
\mathcal{G}=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\mathcal{F}_{i}
. Then, we have
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{a}}
and
\Gamma(Y\to X,\mathcal{F})=\Gamma(Y\to X,\mathcal{G})=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\Gamma(Y\to X,\mathcal{F}_{i})
.
∎

7.10
.

Let
(Y_{\lambda}\to X_{\lambda})_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
be a
\mathbb{U}
-small directed inverse system of morphisms of
\mathbb{U}
-small coherent schemes with affine transition morphisms
Y_{\lambda^{\prime}}\to Y_{\lambda}
and
X_{\lambda^{\prime}}\to X_{\lambda}
(
\lambda^{\prime}\geq\lambda
). We set
(Y\to X)=\lim_{\lambda\in\Lambda}(Y_{\lambda}\to X_{\lambda})
. We regard the directed set
\Lambda
as a filtered category and regard the inverse system
(Y_{\lambda}\to X_{\lambda})_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
as a functor
\varphi:\Lambda^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathbf{E}
from the opposite category of
\Lambda
to the category of morphisms of
\mathbb{U}
-small coherent schemes. Consider the fibred category
\mathbf{E}_{\varphi}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\Lambda^{\mathrm{op}}
defined by
\varphi
whose fibre category over
\lambda
is
\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\lambda}\to X_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
and whose inverse image functor
\varphi_{\lambda^{\prime}\lambda}^{+}:\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\lambda}\to X_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\lambda}^{\prime}\to X_{\lambda}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
associated to a morphism
\lambda^{\prime}\to\lambda
in
\Lambda^{\mathrm{op}}
is given by the base change by the transition morphism
(Y_{\lambda^{\prime}}\to X_{\lambda^{\prime}})\to(Y_{\lambda}\to X_{\lambda})
(cf.
[
AGT16
, VI.11.2]
). Let
\varphi_{\lambda}^{+}:\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\lambda}\to X_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
be the functor defined by the base change by the transition morphism
(Y\to X)\to(Y_{\lambda}\to X_{\lambda})
.

Recall that the filtered colimit of categories
(\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\lambda}\to X_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
is representable by the category
\underrightarrow{\mathbf{E}}_{\varphi}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
whose objects are those of
\mathbf{E}_{\varphi}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
and whose morphisms are given by (
[
SGA 4

II

, VI 6.3, 6.5]
)

(7.10.1)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\underrightarrow{\mathbf{E}}_{\varphi}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}((V\to U),(V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime}))=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(V^{\prime\prime}\to U^{\prime\prime})\to(V\to U)}}{{\textrm{Cartesian}}}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbf{E}_{\varphi}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}((V^{\prime\prime}\to U^{\prime\prime}),(V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime})),

where the colimit is taken over the opposite category of the cofiltered category of Cartesian morphisms with target
V\to U
of the fibred category
\mathbf{E}_{\varphi}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
over
\Lambda^{\mathrm{op}}
(distinguish with the Cartesian morphisms defined in
7.1
).
We see that the functors
\varphi_{\lambda}^{+}
induces an equivalence of categories by
[
EGA IV3
, 8.8.2, 8.10.5]
and
[
EGA IV4
, 17.7.8]

(7.10.2)

\displaystyle\underrightarrow{\mathbf{E}}_{\varphi}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}.

Recall that the cofiltered limit of sites
(\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\lambda}\to X_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
is representable by
\underrightarrow{\mathbf{E}}_{\varphi}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
endowed with the coarsest topology such that the natural functors
\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\lambda}\to X_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\underrightarrow{\mathbf{E}}_{\varphi}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
are continuous (
[
SGA 4

II

, VI.8.2.3]
).

Lemma 7.11
.

With the notation in
7.10
, for any covering family
\mathfrak{U}=\{f_{k}:(V_{k}\to U_{k})\to(V\to U)\}_{k\in K}
in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
with
K
finite, there exists an index
\lambda_{0}\in\Lambda
and a covering family
\mathfrak{U}_{\lambda_{0}}=\{f_{k\lambda_{0}}:(V_{k\lambda_{0}}\to U_{k\lambda_{0}})\to(V_{\lambda_{0}}\to U_{\lambda_{0}})\}_{k\in K}
in
\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\lambda_{0}}\to X_{\lambda_{0}}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
such that
f_{k}
is the base change of
f_{k\lambda_{0}}
by the transition morphism
(Y\to X)\to(Y_{\lambda_{0}}\to X_{\lambda_{0}})
.

Proof.

There is a standard covanishing covering
\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}=\{g_{nm}:(V^{\prime}_{nm}\to U^{\prime}_{n})\to(V\to U)\}_{n\in N,m\in M_{n}}
in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
with
N
,
M_{n}
finite, which refines
\mathfrak{U}
by
6.4
. The equivalence (
7.10.2
) implies that there exists an index
\lambda_{1}\in\Lambda
and families of morphisms
\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}_{\lambda_{1}}=\{g_{nm\lambda_{1}}:(V^{\prime}_{nm\lambda_{1}}\to U^{\prime}_{n\lambda_{1}})\to(V_{\lambda_{1}}\to U_{\lambda_{1}})\}_{n\in N,m\in M_{n}}
(resp.
\mathfrak{U}_{\lambda_{1}}=\{f_{k\lambda_{1}}:(V_{k\lambda_{1}}\to U_{k\lambda_{1}})\to(V_{\lambda_{1}}\to U_{\lambda_{1}})\}_{k\in K}
) in
\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\lambda_{1}}\to X_{\lambda_{1}}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
such that
g_{nm}
(resp.
f_{k}
) is the base change of
g_{nm\lambda_{1}}
(resp.
f_{k\lambda_{1}}
) by the transition morphism
(Y\to X)\to(Y_{\lambda_{1}}\to X_{\lambda_{1}})
and that
\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}_{\lambda_{1}}
refines
\mathfrak{U}_{\lambda_{1}}
. For each
\lambda\geq\lambda_{1}
, let
g_{nm\lambda}:(V^{\prime}_{nm\lambda}\to U^{\prime}_{n\lambda})\to(V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda})
(resp.
f_{k\lambda}:(V_{k\lambda}\to U_{k\lambda})\to(V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda})
) be the base change of
g_{nm\lambda_{1}}
(resp.
f_{k\lambda_{1}}
) by the transition morphism
(Y_{\lambda}\to X_{\lambda})\to(Y_{\lambda_{1}}\to X_{\lambda_{1}})
. Since the morphisms
\coprod_{n\in N}U^{\prime}_{n}\to U
and
\coprod_{m\in M_{n}}V^{\prime}_{nm}\to V\times_{U}U^{\prime}_{n}
are surjective, there exists an index
\lambda_{0}\geq\lambda_{1}
such that the morphisms
\coprod_{n\in N}U^{\prime}_{n\lambda_{0}}\to U_{\lambda_{0}}
and
\coprod_{m\in M_{n}}V^{\prime}_{nm\lambda_{0}}\to V_{\lambda_{0}}\times_{U_{\lambda_{0}}}U^{\prime}_{n\lambda_{0}}
are also surjective by
[
EGA IV3
, 8.10.5]
, i.e.
\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}=\{g_{nm\lambda_{0}}\}_{n\in N,m\in M_{n}}
is a standard covanishing covering in
\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\lambda_{0}}\to X_{\lambda_{0}}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. Thus,
\mathfrak{U}_{\lambda_{0}}=\{f_{k\lambda_{0}}\}_{k\in K}
is a covering family in
\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\lambda_{0}}\to X_{\lambda_{0}}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
.
∎

Proposition 7.12

(
[
AGT16
, VI.11]
)
.

With the notation in
7.10
,
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
represents the limit of sites
(\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\lambda}\to X_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
, and
\overline{\mathscr{B}}=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\varphi_{\lambda}^{-1}\overline{\mathscr{B}}
.

Proof.

The first statement is proved in
[
AGT16
, VI.11.3]
. It also follows directly from the discussion in
7.10
and
7.11
. For the second statement, notice that
\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\varphi_{\lambda}^{-1}\overline{\mathscr{B}}=(\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\varphi_{\lambda,\mathrm{p}}\overline{\mathscr{B}})^{\mathrm{a}}
(
[
Sta22
,
00WI
]
). It suffices to show that
\overline{\mathscr{B}}(V\to U)=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}(\varphi_{\lambda,\mathrm{p}}\overline{\mathscr{B}})(V\to U)
for each object
V\to U
of
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. It follows from the equivalence (
7.10.2
) that there exists an index
\lambda_{0}\in\Lambda
and an object
V_{\lambda_{0}}\to U_{\lambda_{0}}
of
\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\lambda_{0}}\to X_{\lambda_{0}}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
such that
V\to U
is the base change of
V_{\lambda_{0}}\to U_{\lambda_{0}}
by the transition morphism. For each
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}
, let
V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda}
be the base change of
V_{\lambda_{0}}\to U_{\lambda_{0}}
by the transition morphism
(Y_{\lambda}\to X_{\lambda})\to(Y_{\lambda_{0}}\to X_{\lambda_{0}})
. Then, we have
\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}(\varphi_{\lambda,\mathrm{p}}\overline{\mathscr{B}})(V\to U)=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\overline{\mathscr{B}}(V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda})
by
[
SGA 4

II

, VI 8.5.2, 8.5.7]
. The conclusion follows from
\overline{\mathscr{B}}(V\to U)=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\overline{\mathscr{B}}(V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda})
by
3.18
.
∎

Definition 7.13
.

A morphism
X\to S
of coherent schemes is called
pro-étale
(resp.
pro-finite étale
), if there is a directed inverse system of étale (resp. finite étale)
S
-schemes
(X_{\lambda})_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
with affine transition morphisms such that there is an isomorphism of
S
-schemes
X\cong\lim_{\lambda\in\Lambda}X_{\lambda}
. We call such an inverse system
(X_{\lambda})_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
a
pro-étale presentation
(resp.
pro-finite étale presentation
) of
X
over
S
.

Lemma 7.14
.

Let
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\longrightarrow}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\longrightarrow}}S
be morphisms of coherent schemes.

(1)

If
f
is pro-étale (resp. pro-finite étale), then
f
is flat (resp. flat and integral).

(2)

Any base change of a pro-étale (resp. pro-finite étale) morphism is pro-étale (resp. pro-finite étale).

(3)

If
f
and
g
are pro-étale (resp. pro-finite étale), then so is
f\circ g
.

(4)

If
f
and
f\circ g
are pro-étale (resp. pro-finite étale), then so is
g
.

(5)

If
f
is pro-étale with a pro-étale presentation
Y=\lim Y_{\beta}
, and if
g
is étale (resp. finite étale), then there is an index
\beta_{0}
and an étale (resp. finite étale)
S
-morphism
g_{\beta_{0}}:X_{\beta_{0}}\to Y_{\beta_{0}}
such that
g
is the base change of
g_{\beta_{0}}
by
Y\to Y_{\beta_{0}}
.

(6)

Let
Z
and
Z^{\prime}
be coherent schemes pro-étale over
S
with pro-étale presentations
Z=\lim Z_{\alpha}
,
Z^{\prime}=\lim Z^{\prime}_{\beta}
, then

(7.14.1)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{S}(Z,Z^{\prime})=\lim_{\beta}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\alpha}\mathrm{Hom}_{S}(Z_{\alpha},Z^{\prime}_{\beta}).

Proof.

(
1
) and (
2
) follow directly from the definition.

(
3
) We follow closely the proof of
3.6
. Let
X=\lim X_{\alpha}
and
Y=\lim Y_{\beta}
be pro-étale (resp. pro-finite étale) presentations over
Y
and over
S
respectively. As
Y_{\beta}
are coherent, for each
\alpha
, there is an index
\beta_{\alpha}
and an étale (resp. finite étale)
Y_{\beta_{\alpha}}
-scheme
X_{\alpha\beta_{\alpha}}
such that
X_{\alpha}\to Y
is the base change of
X_{\alpha\beta_{\alpha}}\to Y_{\beta_{\alpha}}
(
[
EGA IV3
, 8.8.2, 8.10.5]
,
[
EGA IV4
, 17.7.8]
). For each
\beta\geq\beta_{\alpha}
, let
X_{\alpha\beta}\to Y_{\beta}
be the base change of
X_{\alpha\beta_{\alpha}}\to Y_{\beta_{\alpha}}
by
Y_{\beta}\to Y_{\beta_{\alpha}}
. Then, we have
X=\lim_{\alpha,\beta\geq\beta_{\alpha}}X_{\alpha\beta}
by
[
EGA IV3
, 8.8.2]
(cf.
3.6
), which is pro-finite étale over
S
. For (
5
), one can take
X=X_{\alpha}
.

(
6
) We have

(7.14.2)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{S}(Z,Z^{\prime})=\lim_{\beta}\mathrm{Hom}_{S}(Z,Z^{\prime}_{\beta})=\lim_{\beta}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\alpha}\mathrm{Hom}_{S}(Z_{\alpha},Z^{\prime}_{\beta})

where the first equality follows from the universal property of limits of schemes, and the second follows from the fact that
Z^{\prime}_{\beta}\to S
is locally of finite presentation (
[
EGA IV3
, 8.14.2]
). For (
4
), we take
Z=X
and
Z^{\prime}=Y
. Then, for each index
\beta
, we have an
S
-morphism
X_{\alpha}\to Y_{\beta}
for
\alpha
big enough, which is also étale (resp. finite étale). Then,
X=\lim_{\alpha}X_{\alpha}=\lim_{\alpha,\beta}X_{\alpha}\times_{Y_{\beta}}Y
is pro-étale (resp. pro-finite étale) over
Y
.
∎

Remark 7.15
.

A pro-étale (resp. pro-finite étale) morphism of
\mathbb{U}
-small coherent schemes
X\to S
admits a
\mathbb{U}
-small pro-étale (resp. pro-finite étale) presentation. Indeed, let
X=\lim_{\lambda\in\Lambda}X_{\lambda}
be a presentation of
X\to S
. We may regard
\Lambda
as a filtered category with an initial object
0
. Consider the category
\mathscr{C}={}_{X\backslash}X_{0,\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathrm{aff}}
(resp.
\mathscr{C}={}_{X\backslash}X_{0,\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}
) of affine (resp. finite) étale
X_{0}
-schemes which are under
X
. Notice that
\mathscr{C}
is essentially
\mathbb{U}
-small and that the natual functor
\Lambda\to\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
is cofinal by
7.14
.(
6
) (
[
SGA 4

I

, I.8.1.3]
). Therefore, after replacing
\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
by a
\mathbb{U}
-small directed set
\Lambda^{\prime}
, we obtain a
\mathbb{U}
-small presentation
X=\lim_{X^{\prime}\in\Lambda^{\prime}}X^{\prime}
(
[
SGA 4

I

, I.8.1.6]
).

Definition 7.16
.

For any
\mathbb{U}
-small coherent scheme
X
, we endow the category of
\mathbb{U}
-small coherent pro-étale (resp. pro-finite étale)
X
-schemes with the topology generated by the pretopology formed by families of morphisms

(7.16.1)

\displaystyle\{f_{i}:U_{i}\to U\}_{i\in I}

such that
I
is finite and that
U=\bigcup f_{i}(U_{i})
.
This defines a site
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
(resp.
X_{\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}}
), called the
pro-étale site
(resp.
pro-finite étale site
) of
X
.

It is clear that the localization
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}/U}
(resp.
X_{\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}/U}
) of
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
(resp.
X_{\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}}
) at an object
U
is canonically equivalent to the pro-étale (resp. pro-finite étale) site
U_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
(resp.
U_{\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}}
) of
U
. By definition, any object in
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
(resp.
X_{\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}}
) is quasi-compact.

7.17
.

We compare our definitions of pro-étale site and pro-finite étale site with some other definitions existing in the literature. But we don’t use the comparison result in this paper.

Let
X
be a
\mathbb{U}
-small Noetherian scheme. Consider the category of pro-objects
\mathrm{pro}\textrm{-}X_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}
of
X_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}
, i.e. the category whose objects are functors
F:\mathcal{A}\to X_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}
with
\mathcal{A}
a
\mathbb{U}
-small cofiltered category and whose morphisms are given by
\mathrm{Hom}(F,G)=\lim_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\mathrm{Hom}(F(\alpha),G(\beta))
for any
F:\mathcal{A}\to X_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}
and
G:\mathcal{B}\to X_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}
(
[
Sch13a
, 3.2]
). We may simply denote such a functor
F
by
(X_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in A}
. Remark that
\lim_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}X_{\alpha}
exists which is pro-finite étale over
X
. Consider the functor

(7.17.1)

\displaystyle\mathrm{pro}\textrm{-}X_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}\longrightarrow X_{\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}},\ (X_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in A}\mapsto\lim_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}X_{\alpha},

which is well-defined and fully faithful by
7.14
.(
6
) and essentially surjective by
7.15
. Thus, according to
[
Sch13a
, 3.3]
and its corrigendum
[
Sch16
]
, Scholze’s pro-finite étale site
X_{\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{S}}
has the underlying category
X_{\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}}
and its topology is generated by the families of morphisms

(7.17.2)

\displaystyle\{U_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{i}}}{{\longrightarrow}}U^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\longrightarrow}}U\}_{i\in I}

where
I
is finite and
\coprod_{i\in I}U_{i}\to U^{\prime}
is finite étale surjective, and there exists a well-ordered directed set
\Lambda
with a least index
0
and a pro-finite étale presentation
(U^{\prime}_{\lambda})_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
of
f
such that
U^{\prime}_{0}=U
and that for each
\lambda\in\Lambda
the natural morphism
U^{\prime}_{\lambda}\to\lim_{\mu<\lambda}U^{\prime}_{\mu}
is finite étale surjective (cf.
[
Ker16
, 5.5]
,
7.14
and
[
EGA IV3
, 8.10.5.(vi)]
). It is clear that the topology of our pro-finite étale site
X_{\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}}
is finer than that of
X_{\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{S}}
. We remark that if
X
is connected, then
X_{\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{S}}
gives a site-theoretic interpretation of the continuous group cohomology of the fundamental group of
X
(
[
Sch13a
, 3.7]
). For simplicity, we don’t consider
X_{\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{S}}
in the rest of the paper, but we can replace
X_{\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}}
by
X_{\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{S}}
for most of the statements in this paper (cf.
[
Ker16
, 6]
).

7.18
.

Let
X
be a
\mathbb{U}
-small scheme. Bhatt-Scholze’s pro-étale site
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{BS}}
has the underlying category of
\mathbb{U}
-small weakly étale
X
-schemes and a family of morphisms
\{f_{i}:Y_{i}\to Y\}_{i\in I}
in
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{BS}}
is a covering if and only if for any affine open subscheme
U
of
Y
, there exists a map
a:\{1,\dots,n\}\to I
and affine open subschemes
U_{j}
of
Y_{a(j)}
(
j=1,\dots,n
) such that
U=\bigcup_{j=1}^{n}f_{a(j)}(U_{j})
(
[
BS15
, 4.1.1]
, cf.
[
Sta22
,
0989
]
). Remark that a pro-étale morphism of coherent schemes is weakly étale by
[
BS15
, 2.3.3.1]
. Thus, for a coherent scheme
X
,
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
is a full subcategory of
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{BS}}
.

Lemma 7.19
.

Let
X
be a coherent scheme. The full subcategory
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
of
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{BS}}
is a topologically generating family, and the induced topology on
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
coincides the topology defined in
7.16
. In particular, the topoi of sheaves of
\mathbb{V}
-small sets associated to the two sites are naturally equivalent.

Proof.

For a weakly étale
X
-scheme
Y
, we show that it can be covered by pro-étale
X
-schemes. After replacing
X
by a finite affine open covering and replacing
Y
by an affine open covering, we may assume that
X
and
Y
are affine. Then, the result follows from the fact that for any weakly étale morphism of rings
A\to B
there exists a faithfully flat ind-étale morphism
B\to C
such that
A\to C
is ind-étale by
[
BS15
, 2.3.4]
(cf.
[
BS15
, 4.1.3]
). Thus,
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
is a topologically generating family of
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{BS}}
. A family of morphisms
\{f_{i}:Y_{i}\to Y\}_{i\in I}
in
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
is a covering with respect to the induced topology if and only if for any affine open subscheme
U
of
Y
, there exists a map
a:\{1,\dots,n\}\to I
and affine open subschemes
U_{j}
of
Y_{a(j)}
(
j=1,\dots,n
) such that
U=\bigcup_{j=1}^{n}f_{a(j)}(U_{j})
(
[
SGA 4

I

, III.3.3]
). Notice that
Y_{i}
and
Y
are coherent, thus
\{f_{i}\}_{i\in I}
is a covering if and only if there exists a finite subset
I_{0}\subseteq I
such that
Y=\bigcup_{i\in I_{0}}f_{i}(Y_{i})
, which shows that the induced topology on
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
coincides the topology defined in
7.16
. Finally, the “in particular” part follows from
[
SGA 4

I

, III.4.1]
.
∎

Definition 7.20
.

Let
Y\to X
be a morphism of coherent schemes. A morphism
(V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime})\to(V\to U)
in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}
is called
pro-étale
if
U^{\prime}\to U
is pro-étale and
V^{\prime}\to V\times_{U}U^{\prime}
is pro-finite étale. A
pro-étale presentation
of such a morphism is a directed inverse system
(V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda})_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
étale over
V\to U
with affine transition morphisms
U_{\lambda^{\prime}}\to U_{\lambda}
and
V_{\lambda^{\prime}}\to V_{\lambda}
(
\lambda^{\prime}\geq\lambda
) such that
(V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime})=\lim_{\lambda\in\Lambda}(V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda})
.

Lemma 7.21
.

Let
Y\to X
be a morphism of coherent schemes,
(V^{\prime\prime}\to U^{\prime\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\longrightarrow}}(V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\longrightarrow}}(V\to U)
morphisms in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}
.

(1)

If
f
is pro-étale, then it admits a pro-étale presentation.

(2)

If
f
is pro-étale, then any base change of
f
is also pro-étale.

(3)

If
f
and
g
are pro-étale, then
f\circ g
is also pro-étale.

(4)

If
f
and
f\circ g
are pro-étale, then
g
is also pro-étale.

Proof.

It follows directly from
7.14
and its arguments.
∎

Remark 7.22
.

Similar to
7.15
, a pro-étale morphism in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}
admits a
\mathbb{U}
-small presentation.

7.23
.

Let
Y\to X
be a morphism of coherent schemes,
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
the full subcategory of
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}
formed by objects which are pro-étale over the final object
Y\to X
. It is clear that
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
is stable under finite limits in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}
. Then, the functor (
7.1.5
) induces a functor

(7.23.1)

\displaystyle\phi^{+}:\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\longrightarrow X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\ (V\to U)\longmapsto U,

which endows
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}/X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
with a structure of fibred sites, whose fibre over
U
is the pro-finite étale site
U_{Y,\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}}
. We endow
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
with the associated covanishing topology, that is, the topology generated by the following types of families of morphisms

(v)

\{(V_{m}\to U)\to(V\to U)\}_{m\in M}
, where
M
is a finite set and
\coprod_{m\in M}V_{m}\to V
is surjective;

(c)

\{(V\times_{U}{U_{n}}\to U_{n})\to(V\to U)\}_{n\in N}
, where
N
is a finite set and
\coprod_{n\in N}U_{n}\to U
is surjective.

It is clear that any object in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
is quasi-compact by
6.4
. We still denote by
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
the restriction of the presheaf
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}
to
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
if there is no ambiguity. We will show in
7.32
that
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
is a sheaf on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
.

Definition 7.24
.

We call
(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\overline{\mathscr{B}})
the
pro-étale Faltings ringed site
of the morphism of coherent schemes
Y\to X
.

It is clear that the localization
(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})_{/(V\to U)}
of
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
at an object
V\to U
is canonically equivalent to the pro-étale Faltings ringed site
\mathbf{E}_{V\to U}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
of the morphism
V\to U
by
6.4
.

Remark 7.25
.

The categories
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
,
X_{\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}}
and
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
are essentially
\mathbb{V}
-small categories.

7.26
.

Let
Y\to X
be a morphism of coherent schemes. Consider the natural functors

(7.26.1)

\displaystyle\psi^{+}

\displaystyle:\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\longrightarrow Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\ (V\to U)\longmapsto V,

(7.26.2)

\displaystyle\beta^{+}

\displaystyle:Y_{\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}}\longrightarrow\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\ V\longmapsto(V\to X),

(7.26.3)

\displaystyle\sigma^{+}

\displaystyle:X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\longrightarrow\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\ U\longmapsto(Y\times_{X}U\to U).

They are left exact and continuous (cf.
7.8
). Then, we obtain a commutative diagram of sites associated functorially to the morphism
Y\to X
by
2.5
,

\textstyle{Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{Y_{\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}

where
\rho:Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\to Y_{\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}}
is defined by the inclusion functor, and the unlabelled arrow
Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\to X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
is induced by the morphism
Y\to X
. Moreover, if
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
denotes the structural sheaf on
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
sending
U
to
\Gamma(U,\mathcal{O}_{U})
, then
\sigma^{+}
actually defines a morphism of ringed sites

(7.26.8)

\displaystyle\sigma:(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\overline{\mathscr{B}})\longrightarrow(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}).

Lemma 7.27
.

Let
Y\to X
be a morphism of coherent schemes. Then, the inclusion functor

(7.27.1)

\displaystyle\nu^{+}:\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\longrightarrow\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\ (V\to U)\longmapsto(V\to U)

defines a morphism of sites
\nu:\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
.

Proof.

It is clear that
\nu^{+}
commutes with finite limits and sends a standard covanishing covering in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
to a standard covanishing covering in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
(
6.3
). Therefore,
\nu^{+}
is continuous by
6.4
and defines a morphism of sites.
∎

Lemma 7.28
.

Let
Y\to X
be a morphism of coherent schemes. Then, the topology on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is the topology induced from
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
.

Proof.

After
7.27
, it suffices to show that for a family of morphisms
\mathfrak{U}=\{(V_{k}\to U_{k})\to(V\to U)\}_{k\in K}
in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
, if
\nu^{+}(\mathfrak{U})
is a covering in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
, then
\mathfrak{U}
is a covering in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. We may assume that
K
is finite. There is a standard covanishing covering
\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}=\{(V^{\prime}_{nm}\to U^{\prime}_{n})\to(V\to U)\}_{n\in N,m\in M_{n}}
in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
with
N
,
M_{n}
finite, which refines
\nu^{+}(\mathfrak{U})
by
6.4
. We take a directed set
\Xi
such that for each
n\in N
, we can take a pro-étale presentation
U^{\prime}_{n}=\lim_{\xi\in\Xi}U^{\prime}_{n\xi}
over
U
, and we take a directed set
\Sigma
such that for each
n\in N
and
m\in M_{n}
, we can take a pro-finite étale presentation
V^{\prime}_{nm}=\lim_{\sigma\in\Sigma}V^{\prime}_{nm\sigma}
over
V\times_{U}U^{\prime}_{n}
. By
7.14
(
5
), for each
\sigma\in\Sigma
, there exists an index
\xi_{\sigma}\in\Xi
and a finite étale morphism
V^{\prime}_{nm\sigma\xi_{\sigma}}\to V\times_{U}U^{\prime}_{n\xi_{\sigma}}
for each
n
and
m
, whose base change by
U^{\prime}_{n}\to U^{\prime}_{n\xi_{\sigma}}
is
V^{\prime}_{nm\sigma}\to V\times_{U}U^{\prime}_{n}
. Let
V^{\prime}_{nm\sigma\xi}\to V\times_{U}U^{\prime}_{n\xi}
be the base change of
V^{\prime}_{nm\sigma\xi_{\sigma}}\to V\times_{U}U^{\prime}_{n\xi_{\sigma}}
by the transition morphism
U^{\prime}_{n\xi}\to U^{\prime}_{n\xi_{\sigma}}
for each
\xi\geq\xi_{\sigma}
. Since
\coprod_{m\in M_{n}}V^{\prime}_{nm\sigma}\to V\times_{U}U^{\prime}_{n}
is surjective, after enlarging
\xi_{\sigma}
, we may assume that
\coprod_{m\in M_{n}}V^{\prime}_{nm\sigma\xi}\to V\times_{U}U^{\prime}_{n\xi}
is also surjective for
\xi\geq\xi_{\sigma}
by
[
EGA IV3
, 8.10.5.(vi)]
. It is clear that
\coprod_{n\in N}U^{\prime}_{n\xi}\to U
is surjective for each
\xi\in\Xi
. Therefore,
\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}_{\sigma\xi}=\{(V^{\prime}_{nm\sigma\xi}\to U^{\prime}_{n\xi})\to(V\to U)\}_{n\in N,m\in M_{n}}
is a standard covanishing covering in
\mathbf{E}_{V\to U}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
for each
\sigma\in\Sigma
and
\xi\geq\xi_{\sigma}
. Notice that for each
n\in N
and
m\in M_{n}
, there exists
k\in K
such that the morphism
(V^{\prime}_{nm\sigma\xi}\to U^{\prime}_{n\xi})\to(V\to U)
factors through
(V_{k}\to U_{k})
for
\sigma,\xi
big enough by
7.14
(
6
), which shows that
\mathfrak{U}
is a covering in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
.
∎

Lemma 7.29
.

Let
Y\to X
be a morphism of coherent schemes,
\mathfrak{U}=\{(V_{k}\to U_{k})\to(V\to U)\}_{k\in K}
a covering in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
with
K
finite. Then, there exist pro-étale presentations
(V\to U)=\lim_{\lambda\in\Lambda}(V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda})
,
(V_{k}\to U_{k})=\lim_{\lambda\in\Lambda}(V_{k\lambda}\to U_{k\lambda})
over
Y\to X
and compatible étale morphisms
(V_{k\lambda}\to U_{k\lambda})\to(V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda})
such that the family
\mathfrak{U}_{\lambda}=\{(V_{k\lambda}\to U_{k\lambda})\to(V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda})\}_{k\in K}
is a covering in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
.

Proof.

We follow closely the proof of
3.6
. We take a directed set
A
such that for each
k\in K
we can take a pro-étale presentation
(V_{k}\to U_{k})=\lim_{\alpha\in A}(V_{k\alpha}\to U_{k\alpha})
over
(V\to U)
. Then,
\mathfrak{U}_{\alpha}=\{(f_{k\alpha}:V_{k\alpha}\to U_{k\alpha})\to(V\to U)\}_{k\in K}
is a covering family in
\mathbf{E}_{V\to U}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
for each
\alpha\in A
by
7.28
.

Let
(V\to U)=\lim_{\beta\in B}(V_{\beta}\to U_{\beta})
be a pro-étale presentation over
Y\to X
. For each
\alpha\in A
, there exists an index
\beta_{\alpha}\in B
and a covering family
\mathfrak{U}_{\alpha\beta_{\alpha}}=\{f_{k\alpha\beta_{\alpha}}:(V_{k\alpha\beta_{\alpha}}\to U_{k\alpha\beta_{\alpha}})\to(V_{\beta_{\alpha}}\to U_{\beta_{\alpha}})\}_{k\in K}
such that
f_{k\alpha}
is the base change of
f_{k\alpha\beta_{\alpha}}
by the transition morphism
(V\to U)\to(V_{\beta_{\alpha}}\to U_{\beta_{\alpha}})
(
7.11
). For each
\beta\geq\beta_{\alpha}
, let
f_{k\alpha\beta}:(V_{k\alpha\beta}\to U_{k\alpha\beta})\to(V_{\beta}\to U_{\beta})
be the base change of
f_{k\alpha\beta_{\alpha}}
by the transition morphism
(V_{\beta}\to U_{\beta})\to(V_{\beta_{\alpha}}\to U_{\beta_{\alpha}})
. We take
\Lambda=\{(\alpha,\beta)\in A\times B\ |\ \beta\geq\beta_{\alpha}\}
,
(V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda})=(V_{\beta}\to U_{\beta})
and
(V_{k\lambda}\to U_{k\lambda})=(V_{k\alpha\beta}\to U_{k\alpha\beta})
for each
\lambda=(\alpha,\beta)\in\Lambda
. Then, the families
\mathfrak{U}_{\lambda}=\{(V_{k\lambda}\to U_{k\lambda})\to(V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda})\}_{k\in K}
meet the requirements in the lemma (cf.
3.6
).
∎

Lemma 7.30
.

Let
Y\to X
be a morphism of coherent schemes,
\mathcal{F}
a presheaf on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
,
V\to U
an object of
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
with a pro-étale presentation
(V\to U)=\lim(V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda})
. Then, we have
\nu_{\mathrm{p}}\mathcal{F}(V\to U)=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\mathcal{F}(V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda})
, where
\nu^{+}:\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
is the inclusion functor.

Proof.

Notice that the presheaf
\mathcal{F}
is a filtered colimit of representable presheaves by
[
SGA 4

I

, I.3.4]

(7.30.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{(V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime})\in(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})_{/\mathcal{F}}}h_{V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}.

Thus, we may assume that
\mathcal{F}
is representable by
V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime}
since the section functor
\Gamma(V\to U,-)
commutes with colimits of presheaves (
[
Sta22
,
00VB
]
). Then, we have

(7.30.2)

\displaystyle\nu_{\mathrm{p}}h_{V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(V\to U)=

\displaystyle h_{V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(V\to U)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}((V\to U),(V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}((V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda}),(V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}h_{V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda})

where the first equality follows from
[
Sta22
,
04D2
]
, and the third equality follows from
[
EGA IV3
, 8.14.2]
since
U^{\prime}
and
V^{\prime}
are locally of finite presentation over
X
and
Y\times_{X}U^{\prime}
respectively.
∎

Proposition 7.31
.

Let
Y\to X
be a morphism of coherent schemes,
\mathcal{F}
an abelian sheaf on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
,
V\to U
an object of
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
with a pro-étale presentation
(V\to U)=\lim(V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda})
. Then, for any integer
q
, we have

(7.31.1)

\displaystyle H^{q}(\mathbf{E}_{V\to U}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\nu^{-1}\mathcal{F})=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}H^{q}(\mathbf{E}_{V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathcal{F}),

where
\nu:\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is the morphism of sites defined by the inclusion functor
(
7.27
)
.
In particular, the canonical morphism
\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}\nu_{*}\nu^{-1}\mathcal{F}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We follow closely the proof of
3.8
.
For the second assertion, since
\mathrm{R}^{q}\nu_{*}\nu^{-1}\mathcal{F}
is the sheaf associated to the presheaf
(V\to U)\mapsto H^{q}(\mathbf{E}_{V\to U}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\nu^{-1}\mathcal{F})=H^{q}(\mathbf{E}_{V\to U}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathcal{F})
by the first assertion, which is
\mathcal{F}
if
q=0
and vanishes otherwise.

For the first assertion, we may assume that
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{I}
is an abelian injective sheaf on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
(cf.
3.8
). We claim that for any covering in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
,
\mathfrak{U}=\{(V_{k}\to U_{k})\to(V\to U)\}_{k\in K}
with
K
finite, the augmented Čech complex associated to the presheaf
\nu_{\mathrm{p}}\mathcal{I}
,

(7.31.2)

\displaystyle 0\to\nu_{\mathrm{p}}\mathcal{I}(V\to U)\to\prod_{k}\nu_{\mathrm{p}}\mathcal{I}(V_{k}\to U_{k})\to\prod_{k,k^{\prime}}\nu_{\mathrm{p}}\mathcal{I}(V_{k}\times_{V}V_{k^{\prime}}\to U_{k}\times_{U}U_{k^{\prime}})\to\cdots

is exact. Admitting this claim, we see that
\nu_{\mathrm{p}}\mathcal{I}
is indeed a sheaf, i.e.
\nu^{-1}\mathcal{I}=\nu_{\mathrm{p}}\mathcal{I}
, and the vanishing of higher Čech cohomologies implies that
H^{q}(\mathbf{E}_{V\to U}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\nu^{-1}\mathcal{I})=0
for any
q>0
, which completes the proof together with
7.30
. For the claim, let
(V\to U)=\lim_{\lambda\in\Lambda}(V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda})
and
(V_{k}\to U_{k})=\lim_{\lambda\in\Lambda}(V_{k\lambda}\to U_{k\lambda})
be the pro-étale presentations constructed in
7.29
. The family
\mathfrak{U}_{\lambda}=\{(V_{k\lambda}\to U_{k\lambda})\to(V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda})\}_{k\in K}
is a covering in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. By
7.30
, the sequence (
7.31.2
) is the filtered colimit of the augmented Čech complexes

(7.31.3)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{I}(V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda})\to\prod_{k}\mathcal{I}(V_{k\lambda}\to U_{k\lambda})\to\prod_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathcal{I}(V_{k\lambda}\times_{V_{\lambda}}V_{k^{\prime}\lambda}\to U_{k\lambda}\times_{U_{\lambda}}U_{k^{\prime}\lambda})\to\cdots

which are exact since
\mathcal{I}
is an injective abelian sheaf on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
.
∎

Corollary 7.32
.

With the notation in
7.31
, the presheaf
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
is a sheaf, and the canonical morphisms
\nu^{-1}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\to\overline{\mathscr{B}}
and
\overline{\mathscr{B}}\to\mathrm{R}\nu_{*}\overline{\mathscr{B}}
are isomorphisms. If moreover
p
is invertible on
Y
, then for each integer
n\geq 0
, the canonical morphisms
\nu^{-1}(\overline{\mathscr{B}}/p^{n}\overline{\mathscr{B}})\to\overline{\mathscr{B}}/p^{n}\overline{\mathscr{B}}
and
\overline{\mathscr{B}}/p^{n}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\to\mathrm{R}\nu_{*}(\overline{\mathscr{B}}/p^{n}\overline{\mathscr{B}})
are isomorphisms.

Proof.

For any pro-étale presentation
(V\to U)=\lim(V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda})
, we have
\nu^{-1}\overline{\mathscr{B}}(V\to U)=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}(V_{\lambda}\to U_{\lambda})=\overline{\mathscr{B}}(V\to U)
by
7.30
and
3.18
. This verifies that
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
is a sheaf on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
and that
\nu^{-1}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\to\overline{\mathscr{B}}
is an isomorphism. The second isomorphism follows from the first and
7.31
. For the last assertion, notice that the multiplication by
p^{n}
is injective on
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
, so that the conclusion follows from the exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathscr{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cdot p^{n}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathscr{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathscr{B}}/p^{n}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

∎

7.33
.

We regard the ordered set
\mathbb{N}
of natural numbers as a filtered category (there is an arrow
i\to j
if
i\leq j
). Let
E
be a site. We denote by
E^{\mathbb{N}}
the fibred site
E\times\mathbb{N}
over
\mathbb{N}
, and we endow the category
E^{\mathbb{N}}
with the total topology which makes it into a site without final objects (
6.1
, cf.
[
AGT16
, III.7, VI.7]
). Giving a presheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
E^{\mathbb{N}}
is equivalent to giving a directed inverse system of presheaves
(\mathcal{F}_{n})_{n\geq 0}
on
E
. We write
\mathcal{F}=(\mathcal{F}_{n})_{n\geq 0}
. Moreover,
\mathcal{F}
is a sheaf on
E^{\mathbb{N}}
if and only if each
\mathcal{F}_{n}
is a sheaf on
E
.

7.34
.

Let
Y\to X
be a morphism of coherent schemes. We obtain a fibred site
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}
(resp.
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}
) over
\mathbb{N}
by
7.33
. We define a sheaf
\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}
on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}
(resp.
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}
) by

(7.34.1)

\displaystyle\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}=(\overline{\mathscr{B}}/p^{n}\overline{\mathscr{B}})_{n\geq 0}.

The inclusion functor
\nu^{+}:\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
defines a morphism of ringed fibred sites (
[
SGA 4

II

, VI.7.2.2]
)

(7.34.2)

\displaystyle\breve{\nu}:(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}},\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}})\longrightarrow(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}},\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}),

which induces a morphism of the associated ringed topoi with respect to the total topology (
[
SGA 4

II

, VI.7.4.13.1]
, cf.
[
AGT16
, III.7.18]
).
If moreover the prime number
p
is invertible on
Y
, then the canonical morphisms

(7.34.3)

\displaystyle\breve{\nu}^{-1}\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}\longrightarrow\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}\ \textrm{ and }\ \breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}\breve{\nu}_{*}\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}

are isomorphisms by
7.32
(
[
AGT16
, VI.7.7]
). Combining with the canonical diagrams (
7.8
) and (
7.26
), we obtain a canonical commutative diagram of fibred sites over
\mathbb{N}
,

\textstyle{Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\mathbb{N}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\breve{\psi}}
\scriptstyle{\breve{\nu}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\breve{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\breve{\nu}}
\textstyle{X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\mathbb{N}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\breve{\nu}}
\textstyle{Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\mathbb{N}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\breve{\psi}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\breve{\sigma}}
\textstyle{X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\mathbb{N}}}

where we ambiguously denote by
\breve{\nu}
the morphisms induced by the inclusion functors of the underlying categories. Moreover, if
\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
(resp.
\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
) denotes the sheaf
(\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}})_{n\geq 0}
(resp.
(\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}})_{n\geq 0}
), then the diagram (
7.34
) induces a commutative diagram of ringed fibred sites

\textstyle{(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}},\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\breve{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\breve{\nu}}
\textstyle{(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\mathbb{N}},\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\breve{\nu}}
\textstyle{(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}},\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\breve{\sigma}}
\textstyle{(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\mathbb{N}},\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}})}

Definition 7.35
.

We call
(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}},\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}})
(resp.
(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}},\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}})
) the
p
-adic Faltings ringed site
(resp.
p
-adic pro-étale Faltings ringed site
) of the morphism of coherent schemes
Y\to X
. It is a ringed site without final objects, which can be regarded as a fibred site over
\mathbb{N}
.

8.
Cohomological Descent of the Structural Sheaves

Definition 8.1
.

Let
K
be a pre-perfectoid field of mixed characteristic
(0,p)
,
Y\to X
a morphism of coherent schemes such that
Y\to X^{Y}
is over
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
, where
X^{Y}
denotes the integral closure of
X
in
Y
. We say that
Y\to X
is
Faltings acyclic
if
X
is affine and if for any integer
n>0
the canonical morphism

(8.1.1)

\displaystyle A/p^{n}A\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\overline{\mathscr{B}}/p^{n}\overline{\mathscr{B}})

is an almost isomorphism (cf.
5.7
), where
A
denotes the
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra
\overline{\mathscr{B}}(Y\to X)
(i.e.
X^{Y}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)
).

Remark 8.2
.

In
8.1
, the canonical morphism
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\overline{\mathscr{B}}/p^{n}\overline{\mathscr{B}})\to\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\overline{\mathscr{B}}/p^{n}\overline{\mathscr{B}})
is an isomorphism as (
7.34.3
) are isomorphisms.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
K
be a pre-perfectoid field of mixed characteristic
(0,p)
,
Y\to X
a morphism of coherent schemes such that
Y\to X^{Y}
is over
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
,
A=\overline{\mathscr{B}}(Y\to X)
. Assume that
Y\to X
is Faltings acyclic. Then, we have:

(1)

For any pseudo-uniformizer
\pi
of
K
, the canonical morphism
A/\pi A\to\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\overline{\mathscr{B}}/\pi\overline{\mathscr{B}})
is an almost isomorphism.

(2)

Let
\widehat{A}
be the
p
-adic completion of
A
. Then, the canonical morphism
\widehat{A}\to\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{Y\to X},\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}})
is an almost isomorphism.

Proof.

(
1
) There exists an integer
n>0
such that
\pi^{\prime}=p^{n}/\pi
is a pseudo-uniformizer of
K
. Since
A
and
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
are flat over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, we have a natural morphism of exact sequences

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A/\pi^{\prime}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{1}}
\textstyle{A/p^{n}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{2}}
\textstyle{A/\pi A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{3}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\overline{\mathscr{B}}/\pi^{\prime}\overline{\mathscr{B}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\overline{\mathscr{B}}/p^{n}\overline{\mathscr{B}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\overline{\mathscr{B}}/\pi\overline{\mathscr{B}})}

By definition,
\alpha_{2}
is an almost isomorphism. Thus,
\alpha_{1}
is almost injective. Since any pseudo-uniformizer of
K
is of the form
\pi^{\prime}=p^{n}/\pi
for some pseudo-uniformizer
\pi
of
K
and
n>0
,
\alpha_{3}
is almost injective. By diagram chasing, we see that
\alpha_{1}
is an almost isomorphism (and thus so is
\alpha_{3}
). It remains to show that
H^{q}(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\overline{\mathscr{B}}/\pi\overline{\mathscr{B}})
is almost zero for
q>0
. Since
H^{q}(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\overline{\mathscr{B}}/p^{n}\overline{\mathscr{B}})
is almost zero. By the long exact sequence associated to the short exact sequence
0\to\overline{\mathscr{B}}/\pi^{\prime}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\to\overline{\mathscr{B}}/p^{n}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\to\overline{\mathscr{B}}/\pi\overline{\mathscr{B}}\to 0
, we see that
H^{1}(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\overline{\mathscr{B}}/\pi^{\prime}\overline{\mathscr{B}})
is almost zero and that
H^{q}(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\overline{\mathscr{B}}/\pi\overline{\mathscr{B}})\to H^{q+1}(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\overline{\mathscr{B}}/\pi^{\prime}\overline{\mathscr{B}})
is an almost isomorphism. By induction, we complete the proof.

(
2
) Recall that for any integer
q\geq 0
there exists a canonical exact sequence (
[
AGT16
, VI.7.10]
)

(8.3.5)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathrm{R}^{1}\lim_{n\to\infty}H^{q-1}(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}_{Y\to X},\overline{\mathscr{B}}/p^{n}\overline{\mathscr{B}})\to H^{q}(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{Y\to X},\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}})\to\lim_{n\to\infty}H^{q}(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}_{Y\to X},\overline{\mathscr{B}}/p^{n}\overline{\mathscr{B}})\to 0.

The conclusion follows from the almost isomorphisms (
8.1.1
).
∎

Proposition 8.4
.

Let
K
be a pre-perfectoid field of mixed characteristic
(0,p)
,
Y\to X
a morphism of coherent schemes such that
Y\to X^{Y}
is over
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
,
A=\overline{\mathscr{B}}(Y\to X)
. Assume that
Y\to X
is Faltings acyclic and that
X=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)
with
R
being
p
-torsion free. Let
\mathcal{M}
be an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module of finite presentation with
M=\mathcal{M}(X)
such that
M[1/p]
is a projective
R[1/p]
-module. Then, the canonical morphism

(8.4.1)

\displaystyle M\otimes_{R}\widehat{A}[\frac{1}{p}]\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{Y\to X},\breve{\sigma}^{*}\breve{\mathcal{M}})[\frac{1}{p}]

is an isomorphism, where
\breve{\sigma}:(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{Y\to X},\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}})\to(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\mathbb{N}},\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}})
is defined in
7.34
and
\breve{\mathcal{M}}=\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}}\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}=(\mathcal{M}/p^{n}\mathcal{M})_{n\geq 0}
.

Proof.

Let
N
be the kernel of a surjective
R
-linear homomorphism
\phi:R^{\oplus n}\to M
. We take a splitting
R^{\oplus n}[1/p]=M[1/p]\oplus N[1/p]
. Composing with the inclusion
(M\oplus N)\subseteq(M[1/p]\oplus N[1/p])
, we get an injective map
\widetilde{\phi}:M\oplus N\to R^{\oplus n}[1/p]
. Since
M
and
N
are finitely generated, there exists an integer
k\geq 1
such that
\varphi=p^{k}\widetilde{\phi}:M\oplus N\to R^{\oplus n}\subseteq R^{\oplus n}[1/p]
. It is clear that
\varphi
is injective. We claim that the cokernel of
\varphi
is killed by
p^{2k}
. Indeed, the composition of the maps
M\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi}}{{\longrightarrow}}R^{\oplus n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi}}{{\longrightarrow}}M
is
p^{k}\mathrm{id}_{M}
. Thus, for any
x\in R^{\oplus n}
, the element
y=p^{k}x-\varphi(\phi(x))
of
R^{\oplus n}
lies in
N
. Thus,
p^{2k}x=\varphi(p^{k}\phi(x)+y)
, which proves the claim. Then, there exists an
R
-linear homomorphism
\psi:R^{n}\to M\oplus N
such that
\varphi\circ\psi=p^{4k}\mathrm{id}_{R^{n}}
and
\psi\circ\varphi=p^{4k}\mathrm{id}_{M\oplus N}
(
[
AG20
, 2.6.3]
). Let
\mathcal{N}
be the quasi-coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module associated to
N
. Then, for each integer
q
, the morphism
\varphi
induces an
\widehat{A}
-linear homomorphism

(8.4.2)

\displaystyle H^{q}(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{Y\to X},\breve{\sigma}^{*}\breve{\mathcal{M}})\oplus H^{q}(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{Y\to X},\breve{\sigma}^{*}\breve{\mathcal{N}})\longrightarrow H^{q}(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{Y\to X},\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}^{\oplus n}),

whose kernel and cokernel are killed by
p^{4k}
by the existence of
\psi
. For
q\neq 0
, we have
H^{q}(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{Y\to X},\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}^{\oplus n})[1/p]=0
by
8.3
.(
2
), thus
H^{q}(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{Y\to X},\breve{\sigma}^{*}\breve{\mathcal{M}})[1/p]=0
. For
q=0
, we have
H^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{Y\to X},\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}^{\oplus n})[1/p]=\widehat{A}^{\oplus n}[1/p]
by
8.3
.(
2
). On the other hand, there is a canonical morphism

(8.4.3)

\displaystyle M\otimes_{R}\widehat{A}\oplus N\otimes_{R}\widehat{A}\to H^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{Y\to X},\breve{\sigma}^{*}\breve{\mathcal{M}})\oplus H^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{Y\to X},\breve{\sigma}^{*}\breve{\mathcal{N}})

whose composition with (
8.4.2
) is compatible with
\varphi\otimes_{R}\mathrm{id}_{\widehat{A}}:M\otimes_{R}\widehat{A}\oplus N\otimes_{R}\widehat{A}\to\widehat{A}^{\oplus n}
. Thus,
H^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{Y\to X},\breve{\sigma}^{*}\mathcal{M})[1/p]=M\otimes_{R}\widehat{A}[1/p]
.
∎

Lemma 8.5
.

Let
Y\to X
be a morphism of coherent schemes such that
Y\to X^{Y}
is an open immersion. Then, the functor

(8.5.1)

\displaystyle\epsilon^{+}:\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\longrightarrow\mathbf{I}_{Y\to X^{Y}},\ (V\to U)\longmapsto U^{V},

is well-defined, left exact and continuous. Moreover, we have
Y\times_{X^{Y}}U^{V}=V
.

Proof.

Since
U^{\prime}=X^{Y}\times_{X}U
is integral over
U
, we have
U^{V}=U^{\prime V}
. Applying
3.19
.(
4
) to
V\to U^{\prime}
over
Y\to X^{Y}
, we see that the
X^{Y}
-scheme
U^{V}
is
Y
-integrally closed with
Y\times_{X^{Y}}U^{V}=V
, and thus the functor
\epsilon^{+}
is well-defined. Let
(V_{1}\to U_{1})\to(V_{0}\to U_{0})\leftarrow(V_{2}\to U_{2})
be a diagram in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
. By
3.21
,
U_{1}^{V_{1}}\overline{\times}_{U_{0}^{V_{0}}}U_{2}^{V_{2}}=(U_{1}^{V_{1}}\times_{U_{0}^{V_{0}}}U_{2}^{V_{2}})^{V_{1}\times_{V_{0}}V_{2}}=(U_{1}\times_{U_{0}}U_{2})^{V_{1}\times_{V_{0}}V_{2}}
which shows the left exactness of
\epsilon^{+}
. For the continuity, notice that any covering in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
can be refined by a standard covanishing covering (
6.4
). For a Cartesian covering family
\mathfrak{U}=\{(V\times_{U}U_{n}\to U_{n})\to(V\to U)\}_{n\in N}
with
N
finite, we apply
3.15
to the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\coprod_{n\in N}V\times_{U}U_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\coprod_{n\in N}U_{n}^{V\times_{U}U_{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\coprod_{n\in N}U_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U^{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U}

then we see that
\epsilon^{+}(\mathfrak{U})
is a covering family in
\mathbf{I}_{Y\to X^{Y}}
. For a vertical covering family
\mathfrak{U}=\{(V_{m}\to U)\to(V\to U)\}_{m\in M}
with
M
finite, we apply
3.15
to the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\coprod_{m\in M}V_{m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\coprod_{m\in M}U^{V_{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U^{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U}

then we see that
\epsilon^{+}(\mathfrak{U})
is also a covering family in
\mathbf{I}_{Y\to X^{Y}}
.
∎

8.6
.

Let
Y\to X
be a morphism of coherent schemes such that
Y\to X^{Y}
is an open immersion. Then, there are morphisms of sites

(8.6.1)

\displaystyle\epsilon:\mathbf{I}_{Y\to X^{Y}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},

(8.6.2)

\displaystyle\varepsilon:\mathbf{I}_{Y\to X^{Y}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}

defined by (
8.5.1
) and the composition of (
8.5.1
) with (
7.27.1
) respectively. We temporarily denote by
\mathscr{O}^{\mathrm{pre}}
the presheaf on
\mathbf{I}_{Y\to X^{Y}}
sending
W
to
\Gamma(W,\mathcal{O}_{W})
(thus
\mathscr{O}=(\mathscr{O}^{\mathrm{pre}})^{\mathrm{a}}
). Notice that we have
\overline{\mathscr{B}}=\epsilon^{\mathrm{p}}\mathscr{O}^{\mathrm{pre}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathscr{B}}=\varepsilon^{\mathrm{p}}\mathscr{O}^{\mathrm{pre}}
). The canonical morphism
\epsilon^{\mathrm{p}}\mathscr{O}^{\mathrm{pre}}\to\epsilon^{\mathrm{p}}\mathscr{O}
(resp.
\varepsilon^{\mathrm{p}}\mathscr{O}^{\mathrm{pre}}\to\varepsilon^{\mathrm{p}}\mathscr{O}
) induces a canonical morphism
\overline{\mathscr{B}}\to\epsilon_{*}\mathscr{O}
(resp.
\overline{\mathscr{B}}\to\varepsilon_{*}\mathscr{O}
).

8.7
.

Let
K
be a pre-perfectoid field (
5.1
) of mixed characteristic
(0,p)
,
\eta=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K)
,
S=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
,
Y\to X
a morphism of coherent schemes such that
X^{Y}
is an
S
-scheme with generic fibre
(X^{Y})_{\eta}=Y
. In particular,
X^{Y}
is an object of
\mathbf{I}_{\eta\to S}
.

Lemma 8.8
.

For any ring
R
, there is an
R
-algebra
R_{\infty}
satisfying the following conditions:

(i)

The scheme
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(R_{\infty}[1/p])
is pro-finite étale and faithfully flat over
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(R[1/p])
.

(ii)

The
R
-algebra
R_{\infty}
is the integral closure of
R
in
R_{\infty}[1/p]
.

(iii)

Any unit
t
of
R_{\infty}
admits a
p
-th root
t^{1/p}
in
R_{\infty}
.

Moreover, if
p
lies in the Jacobson radical
J(R)
of
R
, and if there is a
p^{2}
-th root
p_{2}\in R
of
p
up to a unit, and we write
p_{1}=p_{2}^{p}
, then we may require further that

(iv)

the Frobenius of
R_{\infty}/pR_{\infty}
induces an isomorphism
R_{\infty}/p_{1}R_{\infty}\to R_{\infty}/pR_{\infty}
.

Proof.

Setting
B_{0}=R[1/p]
, we construct inductively a ring
B_{n+1}
ind-finite étale over
B_{n}
and we denote by
R_{n}
the integral closure of
R
in
B_{n}
. For
n\geq 0
, we set

(8.8.1)

\displaystyle B_{n+1}=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{T\subseteq R_{n}^{\times}}\bigotimes\nolimits_{B_{n}}^{t\in T}B_{n}[X]/(X^{p}-t)

where the colimit runs through all finite subsets
T
of the subset
R_{n}^{\times}
of units of
R_{n}
and the transition maps are given by the inclusion relation of these finite subsets
T
. Notice that
B_{n}[X]/(X^{p}-t)
is finite étale and faithfully flat over
B_{n}
, thus
B_{n+1}
is ind-finite étale and faithfully flat over
B_{n}
. Now we take
B_{\infty}=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n}B_{n}
. The integral closure
R_{\infty}
of
R
in
B_{\infty}
is equal to
\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n}R_{n}
by
3.18
. We claim that
R_{\infty}
satisfies the first three conditions. Firstly, we see inductively that
B_{n}=R_{n}[1/p]
(
0\leq n\leq\infty
) by
3.17
. Thus, (i), (ii) follow immediately. For (iii), notice that we have
R_{\infty}^{\times}=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n}R_{n}^{\times}
. For an unit
t\in R_{\infty}^{\times}
, we suppose that it is the image of
t_{n}\in R_{n}^{\times}
. By construction, there exists an element
x_{n+1}\in R_{n+1}
such that
x_{n+1}^{p}=t_{n}
. Thus,
t
admits a
p
-th root in
R_{\infty}
.

For (iv), the injectivity follows from the fact that
R_{\infty}
is integrally closed in
R_{\infty}[1/p]
(cf.
5.21
). For the surjectivity, let
a\in R_{\infty}
. Firstly, since
R_{\infty}
is integral over
R
,
p
also lies in the Jacobson radical
J(R_{\infty})
of
R_{\infty}
. Thus,
1+p_{1}a\in R_{\infty}^{\times}
, and then by (iii) there is
b\in R_{\infty}
such that
b^{p}=1+p_{1}a
. We write
(b-1)^{p}=p_{1}a^{\prime}
for some
a^{\prime}\in a+p_{1}R_{\infty}
. Thus,
1+a^{\prime}-a\in R_{\infty}^{\times}
, and then by (iii) there is
c\in R_{\infty}
such that
c^{p}=1+a^{\prime}-a
. On the other hand, since
R_{\infty}
is integrally closed in
R_{\infty}[1/p]
, we have
x=(b-1)/p_{2}\in R_{\infty}
. Now we have
(x-c+1)^{p}\equiv x^{p}-c^{p}+1\equiv a\ (\textrm{mod }pR_{\infty})
, which completes the proof.
∎

Remark 8.9
.

In
8.8
, it follows from the construction that
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(R_{\infty}[1/p])\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(R[1/p])
is a covering in
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(R[1/p])_{\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{S}}
(
7.17
).

Proposition 8.10
.

With the notation in
8.7
, for any object
V\to U
in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
, there exists a covering
\{(V_{i}\to U_{i})\to(V\to U)\}_{i\in I}
with
I
finite such that for each
i\in I
,
U_{i}^{V_{i}}
is the spectrum of an
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra which is almost pre-perfectoid
(
5.19
)
.

Proof.

After replacing
U
by an affine open covering, we may assume that
U=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)
. Consider the category
\mathscr{C}
of étale
A
-algebras
B
such that
A/pA\to B/pB
is an isomorphism, and the colimit
A^{\mathrm{h}}=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}B
over
\mathscr{C}
. In fact,
\mathscr{C}
is filtered and
(A^{\mathrm{h}},pA^{\mathrm{h}})
is the Henselization of the pair
(A,pA)
(cf.
[
Sta22
,
0A02
]
). It is clear that
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A^{\mathrm{h}})\coprod\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A[1/p])\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)
is a covering in
U_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
. So we reduce to the situation where
p\in J(A)
or
p\in A^{\times}
. The latter case is trivial, since the
p
-adic completion of
R=\Gamma(U^{V},\mathcal{O}_{U^{V}})
is zero if
p
is invertible in
A
. Therefore, we may assume that
p\in J(A)
in the following.

Since
R=\Gamma(U^{V},\mathcal{O}_{U^{V}})
is integral over
A
, we also have
p\in J(R)
. Applying
8.8
to the
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra
R
, we obtain a covering
V_{\infty}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(R_{\infty}[1/p])\to V=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(R[1/p])
in
V_{\mathrm{prof\acute{e}t}}
such that
R_{\infty}=\Gamma(U^{V_{\infty}},\mathcal{O}_{U^{V_{\infty}}})
is an
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra which is almost pre-perfectoid by
5.4
and
5.20
.
∎

Proposition 8.11
.

With the notation in
8.7
, if
W
is an object of
\mathbf{I}_{\eta\to S}
such that
W
is the spectrum of an
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra which is almost pre-perfectoid, then for any integer
n>0
, the canonical morphism

(8.11.1)

\displaystyle\Gamma(W,\mathcal{O}_{W})/p^{n}\Gamma(W,\mathcal{O}_{W})\to\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{I}_{W_{\eta}\to W},\mathscr{O}/p^{n}\mathscr{O})

is an almost isomorphism
(
5.7
)
.

Proof.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be the full-subcategory of
\mathbf{I}_{\eta\to S}
formed by the spectrums of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebras which are almost pre-perfectoid. It is stable under fibred product by
5.33
,
5.30
and
3.21
, and it forms a topologically generating family for the site
\mathbf{I}_{\eta\to S}
by
8.5
and
8.10
. It suffices to show that for any covering in
\mathbf{I}_{\eta\to S}
,
\mathfrak{U}=\{W_{k}\to W\}_{k\in K}
consisting of objects of
\mathscr{C}
with
K
finite, the augmented Čech complex associated to the presheaf
W\mapsto\Gamma(W,\mathcal{O}_{W})/p^{n}\Gamma(W,\mathcal{O}_{W})
on
\mathbf{I}_{\eta\to S}
(whose associated sheaf is just
\mathscr{O}/p^{n}\mathscr{O}
),

(8.11.2)

\displaystyle 0\to\Gamma(W,\mathcal{O}_{W})/p^{n}\to\prod_{k}\Gamma(W_{k},\mathcal{O}_{W_{k}})/p^{n}\to\prod_{k,k^{\prime}}\Gamma(W_{k}\overline{\times}_{W}W_{k^{\prime}},\mathcal{O}_{W_{k}\overline{\times}_{W}W_{k^{\prime}}})/p^{n}\to\cdots

is almost exact. Indeed, the almost exactness shows firstly that
\Gamma(W,\mathcal{O}_{W})/p^{n}\to H^{0}(\mathbf{I}_{W_{\eta}\to W},\mathscr{O}/p^{n}\mathscr{O})
is an almost isomorphism (cf.
[
Sta22
,
00W1
]
), so that the augmented Čech complex associated to the sheaf
\mathscr{O}/p^{n}\mathscr{O}
is also almost exact. Then, the conclusion follows from the almost vanishing of the higher Čech cohomologies of
\mathscr{O}/p^{n}\mathscr{O}
by
[
Sta22
,
03F9
]
.

We set
R=\Gamma(W,\mathcal{O}_{W})
and
R^{\prime}=\prod_{k\in K}\Gamma(W_{k},\mathcal{O}_{W_{k}})
. They are almost pre-perfectoid, and
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(R^{\prime})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)
is a v-covering by definition. Thus, the almost exactness of (
8.11.2
) follows from
5.33
,
5.30
and
5.35
.
∎

Theorem 8.12
.

With the notation in
8.7
, let
\epsilon:\mathbf{I}_{Y\to X^{Y}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
be the morphism of sites defined in
8.6
. Then, for any integer
n>0
, the canonical morphism

(8.12.1)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathscr{B}}/p^{n}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\to\mathrm{R}\epsilon_{*}(\mathscr{O}/p^{n}\mathscr{O})

is an almost isomorphism in the derived category
\mathbf{D}(\mathcal{O}_{K}\textrm{-}\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}})

(
5.7
)
.

Proof.

Since
\mathrm{R}^{q}\epsilon_{*}(\mathscr{O}/p^{n}\mathscr{O})
is the sheaf associated to the presheaf
(V\to U)\mapsto H^{q}(\mathbf{I}_{V\to U^{V}},\mathscr{O}/p^{n}\mathscr{O})
and any object in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
can be covered by those objects whose image under
\epsilon^{+}
are the spectrums of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebras which are almost pre-perfectoid by
8.10
, the conclusion follows from
8.11
.
∎

Corollary 8.13
.

With the notation in
8.7
, let
V\to U
be an object of
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
such that
U
is affine and that the integral closure
U^{V}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)
is the spectrum of an
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra
A
which is almost pre-perfectoid. Then,
V\to U
is Faltings acyclic.

Proof.

It follows directly from
8.12
and
8.11
.
∎

Corollary 8.14
.

With the notation in
8.7
, let
\varepsilon:\mathbf{I}_{Y\to X^{Y}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
be the morphism of sites defined in
8.6
. Then, for any finite locally constant abelian sheaf
\mathbb{L}
on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
, the canonical morphism

(8.14.1)

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\to\mathrm{R}\varepsilon_{*}(\varepsilon^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{O})

is an almost isomorphism in the derived category
\mathbf{D}(\mathcal{O}_{K}\textrm{-}\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}})

(
5.7
)
.

Proof.

The problem is local on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
, thus we may assume that
\mathbb{L}
is the constant sheaf with value
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
. Then, the conclusion follows from
8.12
and
7.32
.
∎

Remark 8.15
.

In
8.14
, if
\mathbb{L}
is a bounded complex of abelian sheaves on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
with finite locally constant cohomology sheaves, then the canonical morphism
\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\to\mathrm{R}\varepsilon_{*}(\varepsilon^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}\mathscr{O})
is also an almost isomorphism. Indeed, after replacing
\mathbb{L}
by
\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, we may assume that
\mathbb{L}
is a complex of
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
-modules for some integer
n
(
[
Sta22
,
0DD7
]
). Then, there exists a covering family
\{(Y_{i}\to X_{i})\to(Y\to X)\}_{i\in I}
in
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
such that the restriction of
\mathbb{L}
on
\mathbf{E}_{Y_{i}\to X_{i}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is represented by a bounded complex of finite locally constant
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
-modules (
[
Sta22
,
094G
]
). Then, the conclusion follows directly from
8.14
.

Corollary 8.16
.

With the notation in
8.7
, let
Y\to X_{i}
(
i=1,2
) be a morphism of coherent schemes such that
X_{i}^{Y}
is an
S
-scheme with generic fibre
(X_{i}^{Y})_{\eta}=Y
,
\mathbb{L}
a finite locally constant abelian sheaf on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X_{2}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. If there is a morphism
f:X_{1}\to X_{2}
under
Y
such that the natural morphism
g:X_{1}^{Y}\to X_{2}^{Y}
is a separated v-covering and that
g^{-1}(Y)=Y
, and if we denote by
u:\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X_{1}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X_{2}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
the corresponding morphism of sites, then the natural morphism

(8.16.1)

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\to\mathrm{R}u_{*}(u^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}})

is an almost isomorphism.

Proof.

The morphism
u
is defined by the functor
u^{+}:\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X_{2}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X_{1}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
sending
(V\to U_{2})
to
(V\to U_{1})=(V\to X_{1}\times_{X_{2}}U_{2})
. We set
V_{0}=Y\times_{X_{1}}U_{1}=Y\times_{X_{2}}U_{2}
. According to
3.17
,
U_{1}^{V_{0}}\to U_{2}^{V_{0}}
is the base change of
X_{1}^{Y}\to X_{2}^{Y}
by
U_{2}\to X_{2}
, and thus it is a separated v-covering. Notice that
V_{0}
is an open subscheme in both
U_{1}^{V_{0}}
and
U_{2}^{V_{0}}
, and moreover
V_{0}=V_{0}\times_{U_{2}^{V_{0}}}U_{1}^{V_{0}}
. Applying
3.15
to the commutative diagram

\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U_{1}^{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U_{1}^{V_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U_{2}^{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U_{2}^{V_{0}}}

it follows that
U_{1}^{V}\to U_{2}^{V}
is also a separated v-covering. Let
\varepsilon_{i}:\mathbf{I}_{Y\to X_{i}^{Y}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X_{i}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
(
i=1,2
) be the morphisms of sites defined in
8.6
. The sheaf
\mathrm{R}^{q}u_{*}(u^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}})
is associated to the presheaf
(V\to U_{2})\mapsto H^{q}(\mathbf{E}_{V\to U_{1}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},u^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}})
. We have

(8.16.6)

\displaystyle H^{q}(\mathbf{E}_{V\to U_{1}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},u^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}})

\displaystyle\to H^{q}(\mathbf{I}_{V\to U_{1}^{V}},\varepsilon_{1}^{-1}u^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{O})

\displaystyle=H^{q}(\mathbf{I}_{V\to U_{2}^{V}},\varepsilon_{2}^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{O})\leftarrow H^{q}(\mathbf{E}_{V\to U_{2}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}),

where the equality follows from the fact that the morphism of representable sheaves associated to
U_{1}^{V}\to U_{2}^{V}
on
\mathbf{I}_{\eta\to S}
is an isomorphism by
3.24
, and where the two arrows are almost isomorphisms by
8.14
, which completes the proof.
∎

8.17
.

Let
\Delta
be the category formed by finite ordered sets
[n]=\{0,1,\dots,n\}
(
n\geq 0
) with non-decreasing maps (
[
Sta22
,
0164
]
). For a functor from its opposite category
\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}
to the category
\mathbf{E}
of morphisms of coherent schemes sending
[n]
to
Y_{n}\to X_{n}
, we simply denote it by
Y_{\bullet}\to X_{\bullet}
. Then, we obtain a fibred site
\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\bullet}\to X_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
over
\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}
whose fibre category over
[n]
is
\mathbf{E}_{Y_{n}\to X_{n}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
and the inverse image functor
f^{+}:\mathbf{E}_{Y_{n}\to X_{n}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y_{m}\to X_{m}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
associated to a morphism
f:[m]\to[n]
in
\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}
is induced the base change by the morphism
(Y_{m}\to X_{m})\to(Y_{n}\to X_{n})
associated to
f
. We endow
\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\bullet}\to X_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
with the total topology (
6.1
) and call it the
simplicial Faltings site
associated to
Y_{\bullet}\to X_{\bullet}
(
[
Sta22
,
09WE.(A)
]
). The sheaf
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
on each
\mathbf{E}_{Y_{n}\to X_{n}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
induces a sheaf
\overline{\mathscr{B}}_{\bullet}=(\overline{\mathscr{B}})_{[n]\in\mathrm{Ob}(\Delta)}
on
