\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\bullet}\to X_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
with the notation in
6.5
.

For an augmentation
(Y_{\bullet}\to X_{\bullet})\to(Y\to X)
in
\mathbf{E}
(
[
Sta22
,
018F
]
), we obtain an augmentation of simplicial site
a:\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\bullet}\to X_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
(
[
Sta22
,
0D6Z.(A)
]
). We denote by
a_{n}:\mathbf{E}_{Y_{n}\to X_{n}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
the natural morphism induced by
(Y_{n}\to X_{n})\to(Y\to X)
. Notice that for any sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
, we have
a^{-1}\mathcal{F}=(a_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{F})_{[n]\in\mathrm{Ob}(\Delta)}
with the notation in
6.5
(
[
Sta22
,
0D70
]
).

Corollary 8.18
.

With the notation in
8.7
, let
\mathbb{L}
a finite locally constant abelian sheaf on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
,
X_{\bullet}\to X
an augmentation of simplicial coherent scheme. If we set
Y_{\bullet}=Y\times_{X}X_{\bullet}
and denote by
a:\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\bullet}\to X_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
the augmentation of simplicial site, assuming that
X_{\bullet}^{Y_{\bullet}}\to X^{Y}
is a hypercovering in
\mathbf{I}_{\eta\to S}
, then the canonical morphism

(8.18.1)

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\to\mathrm{R}a_{*}(a^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}_{\bullet})

is an almost isomorphism.

Proof.

Let
b:\mathbf{I}_{Y_{\bullet}\to X_{\bullet}^{Y_{\bullet}}}\to\mathbf{I}_{Y\to X^{Y}}
be the augmentation of simplicial site associated to the augmentation of simplicial object
X_{\bullet}^{Y_{\bullet}}\to X^{Y}
of
\mathbf{I}_{\eta\to S}
(
[
Sta22
,
09X8
]
). The functorial morphism of sites
\varepsilon:\mathbf{I}_{Y\to X^{Y}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
defined in
8.6
induces a commutative diagram of topoi (
[
Sta22
,
0D99
]
)

\textstyle{\mathbf{I}_{Y_{\bullet}\to X_{\bullet}^{Y_{\bullet}}}^{\sim}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{b}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\bullet}\to X_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}\sim}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a}
\textstyle{\mathbf{I}_{Y\to X^{Y}}^{\sim}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}\sim}}

We denote by
a_{n}:\mathbf{E}_{Y_{n}\to X_{n}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
and
b_{n}:\mathbf{I}_{Y_{n}\to X_{n}^{Y_{n}}}\to\mathbf{I}_{Y\to X^{Y}}
the natural morphisms of sites. Consider the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{R}a_{*}(a^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes\overline{\mathscr{B}}_{\bullet})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{3}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{L}\otimes\overline{\mathscr{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}\varepsilon_{*}(\varepsilon^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes\mathscr{O})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{4}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}a_{*}\mathrm{R}\varepsilon_{\bullet*}\varepsilon_{\bullet}^{-1}(a^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes\overline{\mathscr{B}}_{\bullet})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{5}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}c_{*}(c^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes\mathscr{O}_{\bullet})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}\varepsilon_{*}\mathrm{R}b_{*}b^{-1}(\varepsilon^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes\mathscr{O})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where
c=a\circ\varepsilon_{\bullet}=\varepsilon\circ b
, and
\alpha_{2}
(resp.
\alpha_{5}
) is induced by the canonical morphism
\varepsilon^{-1}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\to\mathscr{O}
(resp.
\varepsilon_{\bullet}^{-1}\overline{\mathscr{B}}_{\bullet}\to\mathscr{O}_{\bullet}
), and other arrows are the canonical morphisms.

Notice that
\alpha_{2}
is an almost isomorphism by
8.14
, and that
\alpha_{4}
is an isomorphism by
[
Sta22
,
0D8N
]
as
X_{\bullet}^{Y_{\bullet}}\to X^{Y}
is a hypercovering in
\mathbf{I}_{\eta\to S}
. It remains to show that
\alpha_{5}\circ\alpha_{3}
is an almost isomorphism. With the notation in
6.5
, we have

(8.18.10)

\displaystyle a^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes\overline{\mathscr{B}}_{\bullet}

\displaystyle=(a_{n}^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes\overline{\mathscr{B}})_{[n]\in\mathrm{Ob}(\Delta)}\textrm{ and }

(8.18.11)

\displaystyle c^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes\mathscr{O}_{\bullet}

\displaystyle=(\varepsilon_{n}^{-1}a_{n}^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes\mathscr{O})_{[n]\in\mathrm{Ob}(\Delta)}.

Moreover, by
[
Sta22
,
0D97
]
we have

(8.18.12)

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}^{q}\varepsilon_{\bullet*}(c^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes\mathscr{O}_{\bullet})=(\mathrm{R}^{q}\varepsilon_{n*}(\varepsilon_{n}^{-1}a_{n}^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes\mathscr{O}))_{[n]\in\mathrm{Ob}(\Delta)}

for each integer
q
. Therefore,
a^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes\overline{\mathscr{B}}_{\bullet}\to\mathrm{R}\varepsilon_{\bullet*}(c^{-1}\mathbb{L}\otimes\mathscr{O}_{\bullet})
is an almost isomorphism by
8.14
and so is
\alpha_{5}\circ\alpha_{3}
.
∎

Lemma 8.19
.

With the notation in
8.7
, assume that
X^{Y}
is the spectrum of an
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra which is almost pre-perfectoid. Let
V\to U
be an object of
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
with
U
affine. Then,
U^{V}
is the spectrum of an
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra which is almost pre-perfectoid.

Proof.

Consider the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U^{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\times_{X}U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U^{Y\times_{X}U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X^{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})}

Since taking integral closures commutes with étale base change and filtered colimits (
3.17
,
3.18
), all the squares in (
8
) are Cartesian (
3.19
). Notice that
U^{Y\times_{X}U}
is integral over
U
and thus affine. Since
U^{Y\times_{X}U}
is pro-étale over
X^{Y}
, it is the spectrum of an
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra which is almost pre-perfectoid by
5.37
. As
V
is pro-finite étale over
Y\times_{X}U
, by almost purity
5.41
and
5.37
, we see that
U^{V}
is the spectrum of an
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra which is almost pre-perfectoid.
∎

8.20
.

Let
K
be a pre-perfectoid field of mixed characteristic
(0,p)
which contains a compatible system
\{\zeta_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
of primitive roots of unity,
\eta=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K)
,
S=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
,
Y\to X
a morphism of coherent schemes such that
X^{Y}
is an
S
-scheme and that the induced morphism
Y\to X^{Y}
is an open immersion over
\eta\to S
. Remark that the morphism
X^{Y}_{\eta}\to X
over
\eta\to S
is in the situation
8.7
. We assume further that there exist finitely many nonzero divisors
f_{1},\dots,f_{r}
of
\Gamma(X^{Y}_{\eta},\mathcal{O}_{X^{Y}_{\eta}})
such that the divisor
D=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{div}(f_{i})
on
X^{Y}_{\eta}
has support
X^{Y}_{\eta}\setminus Y
and that at each strict henselization of
X^{Y}_{\eta}
those elements
f_{i}
contained in the maximal ideal form a subset of a regular system of parameters (in particular,
D
is a normal crossings divisor on
X^{Y}_{\eta}
, and we allow
D
to be empty, i.e.
r=0
). We set

(8.20.1)

\displaystyle Y_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\lim_{n}Y[T_{1},\dots,T_{r}]/(T_{1}^{n}-f_{1},\dots,T_{r}^{n}-f_{r}),

where the limit is taken over
\mathbb{N}
with the division relation. We see that
Y_{\infty}
is faithfully flat and pro-finite étale over
Y
.

Proposition 8.21

(Abhyankar’s lemma)
.

Under the assumptions in
8.20
and with the same notation, for any finite étale
Y_{\infty}
-scheme
V_{\infty}
, the integral closure
X^{V_{\infty}}_{\eta}
is finite étale over
X^{Y_{\infty}}_{\eta}
.

Proof.

We set
Z=X^{Y}_{\eta}
. Passing to a strict henselization of
Z
where
D
is non-empty, we may assume that
Z
is local and regular and that
f_{1},\dots,f_{r}
(
r\geq 1
) are all contained in the maximal ideal. We set
Y_{n}=Y[T_{1},\dots,T_{r}]/(T_{1}^{n}-f_{1},\dots,T_{r}^{n}-f_{r})
and
Z_{n}=Z[T_{1},\dots,T_{r}]/(T_{1}^{n}-f_{1},\dots,T_{r}^{n}-f_{r})
for any integer
n>0
. Notice that
Z_{n}
is still local and regular (thus isomorphic to
X^{Y_{n}}_{\eta}
) and that
g_{0}=f_{0}^{1/n},\dots,g_{r}=f_{r}^{1/n}
form a subset of a regular system of parameters for
Z_{n}
(see the proof of
[
SGA 1
, XIII.5.1]
). Using
[
EGA IV3
, 8.8.2, 8.10.5]
and
[
EGA IV4
, 17.7.8]
, there exists an integer
n_{0}>0
and a finite étale
Y_{n_{0}}
-scheme
V_{n_{0}}
such that
V_{\infty}=Y_{\infty}\times_{Y_{n_{0}}}V_{n_{0}}
. We set
V_{n}=Y_{n}\times_{Y_{n_{0}}}V_{n_{0}}
for any
n\geq n_{0}
. According to
[
SGA 4

III

, XIII.5.2]
, there exists a multiple
n_{1}
of
n_{0}
such that
Z_{n_{1}}^{V_{n_{1}}}
is finite étale over
Z_{n_{1}}
. As
Z_{\infty}=\lim Z_{n}
is normal,
Z_{\infty}\times_{Z_{n_{1}}}Z_{n_{1}}^{V_{n_{1}}}
is also normal and thus isomorphic to
Z_{\infty}^{V_{\infty}}=X^{V_{\infty}}_{\eta}
, which shows that the latter is also finite étale over
Z_{\infty}=X^{Y_{\infty}}_{\eta}
.
∎

Corollary 8.22
.

Under the assumptions in
8.20
and with the same notation, the natural functor
\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}_{X^{Y_{\infty}}_{\eta}\to X}\to\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}_{Y_{\infty}\to X}
sending
V\to U
to
Y_{\infty}\times_{X^{Y_{\infty}}_{\eta}}V\to U
induces an equivalence of ringed sites
(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}_{Y_{\infty}\to X},\overline{\mathscr{B}})\to(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}_{X^{Y_{\infty}}_{\eta}\to X},\overline{\mathscr{B}})
.

Proof.

For the equivalence of categories, it suffices to show that the induced functor
u^{+}:\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}_{X^{Y_{\infty}}_{\eta}\to X}\to\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}_{Y_{\infty}\to X}
is an equivalence by
7.14
.(
6
). Since

u^{+}
is a morphism of fibred categories over
X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
, it suffices to show that for each object
U
of
X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
, the fibre functor
u^{+}_{U}:U^{Y_{\infty}\times_{X}U}_{\eta,\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}\to(Y_{\infty}\times_{X}U)_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}
induced by
u^{+}
is an equivalence of categories. Notice that if we replace
Y\to X
in
8.20
by
Y\times_{X}U\to U
, then
(Y\times_{X}U)_{\infty}=Y_{\infty}\times_{X}U
. Therefore, the equivalence of categories follows from applying
8.21
to
Y\times_{X}U\to U
.

To show the equivalence of categories identifies their topologies, it suffices to show that it identifies the vertical coverings and Cartesian coverings in
7.23
. For a finite family
\{(V_{m}\to U)\to(V\to U)\}_{m\in M}
in
\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}_{X^{Y_{\infty}}_{\eta}\to X}
, its image in
\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}_{Y_{\infty}\to X}
is
\{(Y_{\infty}\times_{X^{Y_{\infty}}_{\eta}}V_{m}\to U)\to((Y_{\infty}\times_{X^{Y_{\infty}}_{\eta}}V\to U)\}_{m\in M}
. Notice that
Y_{\infty}\times_{X^{Y_{\infty}}_{\eta}}V
is a dense open subset of
V
as
V
is flat over
X^{Y_{\infty}}_{\eta}
(
[
EGA IV2
, 2.3.7]
), and the same holds for
V_{m}
. Thus, the integral morphism
\coprod_{m\in M}V_{m}\to V
is surjective if and only if
\coprod_{m\in M}Y_{\infty}\times_{X^{Y_{\infty}}_{\eta}}V_{m}\to Y_{\infty}\times_{X^{Y_{\infty}}_{\eta}}V
is surjective. On the other hand, it is tautological that the equivalence identifies the Cartesian coverings. Hence, the two sites are naturally equivalent.

The identification of the structural sheaves by the equivalence of sites follows from the fact that
V
is integrally closed in
Y_{\infty}\times_{X^{Y_{\infty}}_{\eta}}V
for any object
V\to U
of
\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}_{X^{Y_{\infty}}_{\eta}\to X}
as
V
is pro-étale over
X^{Y_{\infty}}_{\eta}
(
3.19
).
∎

Corollary 8.23
.

Under the assumptions in
8.20
and with the same notation, let
V\to U
be an object of
\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\infty}\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
such that
U^{V}
is the spectrum of an
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra which is almost pre-perfectoid, and let
V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime}
be an object of
\mathbf{E}_{V\to U}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
with
U^{\prime}
affine. Then,
U^{\prime V^{\prime}}
is the spectrum of an
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra which is almost pre-perfectoid.

Proof.

It follows directly from
8.22
and
8.19
.
∎

Theorem 8.24
.

Under the assumptions in
8.20
and with the same notation, let
V\to U
be an object of
\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\infty}\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
. Then, the following statements are equivalent:

(1)

The morphism
V\to U
is Faltings acyclic.

(2)

The scheme
U
is affine and
U^{V}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)
is the spectrum of an
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra
A
which is almost pre-perfectoid.

Proof.

(
2
)
\Rightarrow
(
1
): Let
V^{\prime}\to U
be an object of
\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}_{X^{Y_{\infty}}_{\eta}\to X}
whose image under the equivalence in
8.22
is isomorphic to
V\to U
. Then,
U^{V^{\prime}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)
,
V^{\prime}=U^{V^{\prime}}_{\eta}
, and
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}_{V\to U},\overline{\mathscr{B}}/p^{n}\overline{\mathscr{B}})=\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}_{V^{\prime}\to U},\overline{\mathscr{B}}/p^{n}\overline{\mathscr{B}})
. The conclusion follows from
8.13
.

(
1
)
\Rightarrow
(
2
): Firstly, notice that the objects
V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime}
of
\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\infty}\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
satisfying the condition (
2
) form a topological generating family by
8.22
and
8.10
. Let
p_{1}\in\mathcal{O}_{K}
be a
p
-th root of
p
up to a unit. Then, we see that the Frobenius induces an almost isomorphism
\overline{\mathscr{B}}/p_{1}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\to\overline{\mathscr{B}}/p\overline{\mathscr{B}}
by evaluating these sheaves at the objects
V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime}
. The Frobenius also induces an almost isomorphism
A/p_{1}A\to A/pA
by
8.3
.(
1
), which shows that
A
is almost pre-perfectoid.
∎

9.
Complements on Logarithmic Geometry

We briefly recall some notions and facts of logarithmic geometry which will be used in the rest of the paper. We refer to
[
Kat89
,
Kat94
,
GR04
,
Ogu18
]
for a systematic development of logarithmic geometry, and to
[
AGT16
, II.5]
for a brief summary of the theory.

9.1
.

We only consider logarithmic structures in étale topology. More precisely, let
X
be a scheme,
X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
the étale site of
X
,
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
the structure sheaf on
X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
,
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}^{\times}
the subsheaf of units of
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
. A logarithmic struture on
X
is a homomorphism of sheaves of monoids
\alpha:\mathscr{M}\to\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
on
X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
which induces an isomorphism
\alpha^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}^{\times})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}^{\times}
. We denote by
(X,\mathscr{M})
the associated logarithmic scheme (cf.
[
AGT16
, II.5.11]
).

9.2
.

Let
(X,\mathscr{M})
be a coherent log scheme (cf.
[
AGT16
, II.5.15]
). Then, there is a maximal open subscheme
X^{\mathrm{tr}}
of
X
on which
\mathscr{M}
is trivial, and moreover it is functorial in
(X,\mathscr{M})
(
[
Ogu18
, III.1.2.8]
). Let
(X,\mathscr{M})\to(S,\mathscr{L})\leftarrow(Y,\mathscr{N})
be a diagram of fine and saturated log schemes (cf.
[
AGT16
, II.5.15]
). Then, the fibred product is representable in the category of fine and saturated log schemes by
(Z,\mathscr{P})=(X,\mathscr{M})\times_{(S,\mathscr{L})}^{\mathrm{fs}}(Y,\mathscr{N})
. We remark that
Z^{\mathrm{tr}}=X^{\mathrm{tr}}\times_{S^{\mathrm{tr}}}Y^{\mathrm{tr}}
, that
Z\to X\times_{S}Y
is finite, and that
Z^{\mathrm{tr}}\to Z
is Cartesian over
X^{\mathrm{tr}}\times_{S^{\mathrm{tr}}}Y^{\mathrm{tr}}\to X\times_{S}Y
(
[
Ogu18
, III 2.1.2, 2.1.6]
). Moreover, if
X^{\mathrm{tr}}=X
, then
Z=X\times_{S}Y
(
[
Ogu18
, III.2.1.3]
).

9.3
.

For an open immersion
j:Y\to X
, we denote by
j_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}:Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
the morphism of their étale sites defined by the base change by
j
. Let
\mathscr{M}_{Y\to X}
be the preimage of
j_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}\mathcal{O}_{Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}^{\times}
under the natural map
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}\to j_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}\mathcal{O}_{Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
, and we endow
X
with the logarithmic structure
\mathscr{M}_{Y\to X}\to\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
, which is called the compactifying log structure associated to the open immersion
j
(
[
Ogu18
, III.1.6.1]
). Sometimes we write
\mathscr{M}_{Y\to X}
as
\mathscr{M}_{X}
if
Y
is clear in the context.

9.4
.

Let
(X,\mathscr{M})
be a fine and saturated log scheme which is regular (
[
Kat94
, 2.1]
,
[
Niz06
, 2.3]
). Then,
X
is locally Noetherian and normal, and
X^{\mathrm{tr}}
is regular and dense in
X
(
[
Kat94
, 4.1]
). Moreover, there is a natural isomorphism
\mathscr{M}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{M}_{X^{\mathrm{tr}}\to X}
(
[
Kat94
, 11.6]
,
[
Niz06
, 2.6]
). We remark that if
X
is a regular scheme with a strict normal crossings divisor
D
, then
(X,\mathscr{M}_{X\setminus D\to X})
is fine, saturated and regular (
[
Ogu18
, III.1.11.9]
).

Let
f:(X,\mathscr{M})\to(S,\mathscr{L})
be a smooth (resp. saturated) morphism of fine and saturated log schemes (cf.
[
AGT16
, II 5.25, 5.18]
). Then,
f
remains smooth (resp. saturated) under the base change in the category of fine and saturated log schemes (
[
Ogu18
, IV.3.1.2, IV.3.1.11]
, resp.
[
Ogu18
, III.2.5.3]
). We remark that if
f
is smooth, then
f^{\mathrm{tr}}:X^{\mathrm{tr}}\to S^{\mathrm{tr}}
is a smooth morphism of schemes. If moreover
(S,\mathscr{L})
is regular, then
(X,\mathscr{M})
is also regular (
[
Ogu18
, IV.3.5.3]
). We also remark that if
f
is saturated, then for any fibred product in the category of fine and saturated log schemes
(Z,\mathscr{P})=(X,\mathscr{M})\times_{(S,\mathscr{L})}^{\mathrm{fs}}(Y,\mathscr{N})
, we have
Z=X\times_{S}Y
(
[
Tsu19
, II.2.13]
).

9.5
.

Let
K
be a complete discrete valuation field with valuation ring
\mathcal{O}_{K}
,
k
the residue field of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
,
\pi
a uniformizer of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
. We set
\eta=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K)
,
S=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
and
s=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(k)
. Then,
(S,\mathscr{M}_{\eta\to S})
is fine, saturated and regular, since
\mathbb{N}\to\Gamma(S,\mathscr{M}_{\eta\to S})
sending
1
to
\pi
forms a chart of
(S,\mathscr{M}_{\eta\to S})
(cf.
[
AGT16
, II.5.13, II.6.1]
). Recall that an open immersion
Y\to X
of quasi-compact and separated schemes over
\eta\to S
is strictly semi-stable (
[
dJ96
, 6.3]
) if and only if the following conditions are satisfied (
[
dJ96
, 6.4]
,
[
EGA IV4
, 17.5.3]
):

(i)

For each point
x
of the generic fibre
X_{\eta}
, there is an open neighborhood
U\subseteq X_{\eta}
of
x
and a smooth
K
-morphism

(9.5.1)

\displaystyle f:U\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K[s_{1},\dots,s_{m}])

such that
f
maps
x
to the point associated to the maximal ideal
(s_{1},\dots,s_{m})
and that
U\setminus Y
is the inverse image of the closed subset defined by
s_{1}\cdots s_{m}=0
.

(ii)

For each point
x
of the special fibre
X_{s}
, there is an open neighborhood
U\subseteq X
of
x
and a smooth
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-morphism

(9.5.2)

\displaystyle f:U\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K}[t_{1},\dots,t_{n},s_{1},\dots,s_{m}]/(\pi-t_{1}\cdots t_{n}))

such that
f
maps
x
to the point associated to the maximal ideal
(t_{1},\dots,t_{n},s_{1},\dots,s_{m})
and that
U\setminus Y
is the inverse image of the closed subset defined by
t_{1}\cdots t_{n}\cdot s_{1}\cdots s_{m}=0
.

We call an open immersion
Y\to X
of quasi-compact and separated schemes over
\eta\to S
is semi-stable if for any point
x
of
X
there is an étale neighborhood
U
of
x
such that
Y\times_{X}U\to U
is strictly semi-stable. In this case,
(X,\mathscr{M}_{Y\to X})
is fine, saturated and regular which is smooth and saturated over
(S,\mathscr{M}_{\eta\to S})
, since for any point
x
of
X
there is an étale neighborhood
U
of
x
such that there exists a chart for the morphism
(U,\mathscr{M}_{Y\times_{X}U\to U})\to(S,\mathscr{M}_{\eta\to S})
subordinate to the morphism
\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}^{n}\oplus\mathbb{N}^{m}
sending
1
to
(1,\dots,1,0,\dots,0)
such that the induced morphism
U\to S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}}\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}^{n}\oplus\mathbb{N}^{m}}
is smooth (cf.
[
Ogu18
, IV.3.1.18]
).

9.6
.

Recall that a morphism of schemes
f:X\to S
is called
generically finite
if there exists a dense open subscheme
U
of
S
such that
f^{-1}(U)\to U
is finite. We remark that for a morphism
f:X\to S
of finite type between Noetherian schemes which maps generic points to generic points,
f
is generically finite if and only if the residue field of any generic point
\eta
of
X
is a finite field extension of the residue field of
f(\eta)
(
[
ILO14
, II.1.1.7]
).

9.7
.

Let
K
be a complete discrete valuation field with valuation ring
\mathcal{O}_{K}
,
L
an algebraically closed valuation field of height
1
extension of
K
with valuation ring
\mathcal{O}_{L}
,
\overline{K}
the algebraic closure of
K
in
L
.

Consider the category
\mathscr{C}
of open immersions between integral affine schemes
U\to T
over
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
under
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(L)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
such that
T
is of finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and that
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(L)\to U
is dominant. Let
\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{car}}
be the full subcategory of
\mathscr{C}
formed by those objects
U\to T
Cartesian over
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
.

\textstyle{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})}

We note that the objects of
\mathscr{C}
are of the form
(U=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(B)\to T=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A))
where
A
(resp.
B
) is a finitely generated
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-subalgebra of
\mathcal{O}_{L}
(resp.
K
-subalgebra of
L
) with
A\subseteq B
such that
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(B)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)
is an open immersion.

Lemma 9.8
.

With the notation in
9.7
, we have:

(1)

The category
\mathscr{C}
is cofiltered, and the subcategory
\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{car}}
is initial in
\mathscr{C}
.

(2)

The morphism
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(L)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
represents the cofiltered limit of morphisms
U\to T
indexed by
\mathscr{C}
in the category of morphisms of schemes
(cf.
7.1
)
.

(3)

There exists a directed inverse system
(U_{\lambda}\to T_{\lambda})_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
of objects of
\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{car}}
over a directed inverse system
(\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K_{\lambda})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\lambda}}))_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
of objects of
\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{car}}
such that
K_{\lambda}
is a finite field extension of
K
in
L
, that
\overline{K}=\bigcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda}K_{\lambda}
, that
U_{\lambda}\to T_{\lambda}
is strictly semi-stable over
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K_{\lambda})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\lambda}})

(
9.5
)
, and that
(U_{\lambda}\to T_{\lambda})_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
forms an initial full subcategory of
\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{car}}
.

Proof.

(1) For a diagram
(U_{1}\to T_{1})\to(U_{0}\to T_{0})\leftarrow(U_{2}\to T_{2})
in
\mathscr{C}
, let
T
be the scheme theoretic image of
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(L)\to T_{1}\times_{T_{0}}T_{2}
and let
U
be the intersection of
U_{1}\times_{U_{0}}U_{2}
with
T
. It is clear that
T
is of finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
as
\mathcal{O}_{K}
is Noetherian, that
U
and
T
are integral and affine, that
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(L)\to U
is dominant, and that
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(L)\to T
factors through
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
. Thus,
U\to T
is an object of
\mathscr{C}
, which shows that
\mathscr{C}
is cofiltered. For an object
(U=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(B)\to T=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A))
of
\mathscr{C}
, we write
\mathcal{O}_{L}
as a filtered union of finitely generated
A
-subalgebras
A_{i}
. Let
\pi
be a uniformizer of
K
. Notice that
L=\mathcal{O}_{L}[1/\pi]=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}A_{i}[1/\pi]
and that
\mathrm{Hom}_{K\textrm{-}\mathbf{Alg}}(B,L)=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{K\textrm{-}\mathbf{Alg}}(B,A_{i}[1/\pi])
by
[
EGA IV3
, 8.14.2.2]
. Thus, there exists an index
i
such that
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A_{i}[1/\pi])\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A_{i})
is an object of
\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{car}}
over
U\to T
.

(2) It follows immediately from the arguments above.

(3) Consider the category
\mathscr{D}
of morphisms of
\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{car}}
,

\textstyle{U^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})}

such that
K^{\prime}
is a finite field extension of
K
. Similarly, this category is also cofiltered with limit of diagrams of schemes
(\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(L)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}))\to(\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\overline{K})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{\overline{K}}))
. It suffices to show that the full subcategory of
\mathscr{D}
formed by strictly semi-stable objects is initial. For any object
U\to T
of
\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{car}}
, by de Jong’s alteration theorem
[
dJ96
, 6.5]
, there exists a proper surjective and generically finite morphism
T^{\prime}\to T
of integral schemes such that
U^{\prime}=U\times_{T}T^{\prime}\to T^{\prime}
is strictly semi-stable over
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K^{\prime})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})
for a finite field extension
K\to K^{\prime}
. Since
L
is algebraically closed, the dominant morphism
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(L)\to U
lifts to a dominant morphism
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(L)\to U^{\prime}
(
9.6
), which further extends to a lifting
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})\to T^{\prime}
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})\to T
by the valuative criterion. After replacing
T^{\prime}
by an affine open neighborhood of the image of the closed point of
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
, we obtain a strictly semi-stable object of
\mathscr{D}
over
(U\to T)\to(\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K}))
, which completes the proof.
∎

Theorem 9.9

(
[
ILO14
, X 3.5, 3.7]
)
.

Let
K
be a complete discrete valuation field with valuation ring
\mathcal{O}_{K}
,
(Y\to X)\to(U\to T)
a morphism of dominant open immersions over
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
between irreducible
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-schemes of finite type such that
X\to T
is proper surjective. Then, there exists a commutative diagram of dominant open immersions between irreducible
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-schemes of finite type

\textstyle{(Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\beta^{\circ},\beta)}
\scriptstyle{(f^{\prime\circ},f^{\prime})}
\textstyle{(Y\to X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f^{\circ},f)}
\textstyle{(U^{\prime}\to T^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\alpha^{\circ},\alpha)}
\textstyle{(U\to T)}

satisfying the following conditions:

(i)

We have
Y^{\prime}=\beta^{-1}(Y)\cap f^{\prime-1}(U^{\prime})
, i.e.
Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}
is Cartesian over
U^{\prime}\times_{U}Y\to T^{\prime}\times_{T}X

(cf.
7.1
)
.

(ii)

The morphism
(X^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}})\to(T^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{U^{\prime}\to T^{\prime}})
induced by
(f^{\prime\circ},f^{\prime})
is a smooth and saturated morphism of fine, saturated and regular log schemes.

(iii)

The morphisms
\alpha
and
\beta
are proper surjective and generically finite, and
f^{\prime}
is projective surjective.

Proof.

We may assume that
T
is nonempty. Recall that
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
is universally
\mathbb{Q}
-resolvable (
[
ILO14
, X.3.3]
) by de Jong’s alteration theorem
[
dJ96
, 6.5]
. Thus,
T
is also universally
\mathbb{Q}
-resolvable by
[
ILO14
, X 3.5, 3.5.2]
so that we can apply
[
ILO14
, X.3.5]
to the proper surjective morphism
f
and the nowhere dense closed subset
X\setminus Y
. Then, we obtain a commutative diagram of schemes

\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{T^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{T}

and dense open subsets
U^{\prime}\subseteq T^{\prime}
,
Y^{\prime}=\beta^{-1}(Y)\cap f^{\prime-1}(U^{\prime})\subseteq X^{\prime}
such that
(X^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}})
and
(T^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{U^{\prime}\to T^{\prime}})
are fine, saturated and regular, that
(X^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}})\to(T^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{U^{\prime}\to T^{\prime}})
is smooth, that
\alpha,\beta
are proper surjective and generically finite morphisms which map generic points to generic points, and that
f^{\prime}
is projective (since
f
is proper, cf.
[
ILO14
, X 3.1.6, 3.1.7]
). Since
X
(resp.
T
) is irreducible and
X^{\prime}
(resp.
T^{\prime}
) is a disjoint union of normal integral schemes (
9.4
), after firstly replacing
X^{\prime}
by an irreducible component and then replacing
T^{\prime}
by the irreducible component under
X^{\prime}
, we may assume that
X^{\prime}
and
T^{\prime}
are irreducible. Then,
Y^{\prime}\to U^{\prime}
is dominant (so that
f^{\prime}
is projective surjective), since it is smooth and
Y^{\prime}
is nonempty (
[
EGA IV2
, 2.3.4]
). We claim that
\alpha
maps
U^{\prime}
into
U
. Indeed, if there exists a point
u\in U^{\prime}
with
\alpha(u)\notin U
, then
f^{\prime-1}(u)\cap Y^{\prime}=\emptyset
. However, endowing
u
with the trivial log structure, the log scheme
(u,\mathcal{O}_{u_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}^{\times})
is fine, saturated and regular, and the fibred product in the category of fine and saturated log schemes

(9.9.9)

\displaystyle(u,\mathcal{O}_{u_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}^{\times})\times_{(T^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{U^{\prime}\to T^{\prime}})}^{\mathrm{fs}}(X^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}})

is regular with underlying scheme
f^{\prime-1}(u)
(
9.4
,
9.2
). Thus,
f^{\prime-1}(u)\cap Y^{\prime}
is dense in
f^{\prime-1}(u)
, which contradicts the assumption that
f^{\prime-1}(u)\cap Y^{\prime}=\emptyset
since
f^{\prime}
is surjective. Thus, we obtain a diagram (
9.9
) satisfying all the conditions except the saturatedness of
(X^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}})\to(T^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{U^{\prime}\to T^{\prime}})
.

To make
(X^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}})\to(T^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{U^{\prime}\to T^{\prime}})
saturated, we apply
[
ILO14
, X.3.7]
to the morphism
(f^{\prime\circ},f^{\prime})
. We obtain a Cartesian morphism
(\gamma^{\circ},\gamma):(U^{\prime\prime}\to T^{\prime\prime})\to(U^{\prime}\to T^{\prime})
of dominant open immersions such that
(T^{\prime\prime},\mathscr{M}_{U^{\prime\prime}\to T^{\prime\prime}})
is a fine, saturated and regular log scheme, that
\gamma
is a proper surjective and generically finite morphism which maps generic points of
T^{\prime\prime}
to the generic point of
T^{\prime}
, and that the fibred product in the category of fine and saturated log schemes

(9.9.10)

\displaystyle(T^{\prime\prime},\mathscr{M}_{U^{\prime\prime}\to T^{\prime\prime}})\times^{\mathrm{fs}}_{(T^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{U^{\prime}\to T^{\prime}})}(X^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}})

is saturated over
(T^{\prime\prime},\mathscr{M}_{U^{\prime\prime}\to T^{\prime\prime}})
. The fibred product (
9.9.10
) is still smooth over
(T^{\prime\prime},\mathscr{M}_{U^{\prime\prime}\to T^{\prime\prime}})
, and thus it is regular (
9.4
). Let
X^{\prime\prime}
be the underlying scheme of it and let
Y^{\prime\prime}=(X^{\prime\prime})^{\mathrm{tr}}
. Then, the fibred product (
9.9.10
) is isomorphic to
(X^{\prime\prime},\mathscr{M}_{Y^{\prime\prime}\to X^{\prime\prime}})
(
9.4
). Thus, we obtain a commutative diagram of dominant open immersions of schemes

\textstyle{(Y^{\prime\prime}\to X^{\prime\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\delta^{\circ},\delta)}
\scriptstyle{(f^{\prime\prime\circ},f^{\prime\prime})}
\textstyle{(Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f^{\prime\circ},f^{\prime})}
\textstyle{(U^{\prime\prime}\to T^{\prime\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\gamma^{\circ},\gamma)}
\textstyle{(U^{\prime}\to T^{\prime})}

Notice that
Y^{\prime\prime}=U^{\prime\prime}\times_{U^{\prime}}Y^{\prime}
and
X^{\prime\prime}\to T^{\prime\prime}\times_{T^{\prime}}X^{\prime}
is finite, and that
Y^{\prime\prime}\to X^{\prime\prime}
is Cartesian over
U^{\prime\prime}\times_{U^{\prime}}Y^{\prime}\to T^{\prime\prime}\times_{T^{\prime}}X^{\prime}
(
9.2
). Thus, we see that
Y^{\prime\prime}\to X^{\prime\prime}
is Cartesian over
U^{\prime\prime}\times_{U}Y\to T^{\prime\prime}\times_{T}X
and that
f^{\prime\prime}
is projective. Since
T^{\prime}
(resp.
X^{\prime}
) is irreducible and
T^{\prime\prime}
(resp.
X^{\prime\prime}
) is a disjoint union of normal integral schemes (
9.4
), after firstly replacing
T^{\prime\prime}
by an irreducible component and then replacing
X^{\prime\prime}
by an irreducible component on which the restriction of
\delta^{\circ}
is dominant, we may assume that
T^{\prime\prime}
and
X^{\prime\prime}
are irreducible. In particular,
\delta
is generically finite and so is
\beta\circ\delta
(
9.6
), and again
Y^{\prime\prime}\to U^{\prime\prime}
is dominant so that
f^{\prime\prime}
is projective surjective.
∎

Lemma 9.10
.

Let
X
be a scheme of finite type over a valuation ring
A
of height
1
. Then, the underlying topological space of
X
is Noetherian.

Proof.

Let
\eta
and
s
be the generic point and closed point of
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)
respectively. Then, the generic fibre
X_{\eta}
and the special fibre
X_{s}
are both Noetherian. As a union of
X_{\eta}
and
X_{s}
, the underlying topological space of
X
is also Noetherian (
[
Sta22
,
0053
]
).
∎

Proposition 9.11
.

With the notation in
9.7
and
9.8
, let
Y\to X
be a quasi-compact dominant open immersion over
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(L)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
such that
X\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
is proper of finite presentation. Then, there exists a proper surjective
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-morphism of finite presentation
X^{\prime}\to X
, an index
\lambda_{1}\in\Lambda
, and a directed inverse system of open immersions
(Y^{\prime}_{\lambda}\to X^{\prime}_{\lambda})_{\lambda\geq\lambda_{1}}
over
(U_{\lambda}\to T_{\lambda})_{\lambda\geq\lambda_{1}}
satisfying the following conditions for each
\lambda\geq\lambda_{1}
:

(i)

We have
Y^{\prime}=Y\times_{X}X^{\prime}=\lim_{\lambda\geq\lambda_{1}}Y^{\prime}_{\lambda}
and
X^{\prime}=\lim_{\lambda\geq\lambda_{1}}X^{\prime}_{\lambda}
.

(ii)

The log scheme
(X^{\prime}_{\lambda},\mathscr{M}_{Y^{\prime}_{\lambda}\to X^{\prime}_{\lambda}})
is fine, saturated and regular.

(iii)

The morphism
(X^{\prime}_{\lambda},\mathscr{M}_{Y^{\prime}_{\lambda}\to X^{\prime}_{\lambda}})\to(T_{\lambda},\mathscr{M}_{U_{\lambda}\to T_{\lambda}})
is smooth and saturated, and
X^{\prime}_{\lambda}\to T_{\lambda}
is projective.

(iv)

If moreover
Y=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(L)\times_{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})}X
, then we can require that
Y^{\prime}_{\lambda}=U_{\lambda}\times_{T_{\lambda}}X^{\prime}_{\lambda}
.

Proof.

We follow closely the proof of
[
ALPT19
, 5.2.19]
. Since the underlying topological space of
X
is Noetherian by
9.10
, each irreducible component
Z
of
X
admits a closed subscheme structure such that
Z\to X
is of finite presentation (
[
Sta22
,
01PH
]
). After replacing
X
by the disjoint union of its irreducible components, we may assume that
X
is irreducible. Then, the generic fibre of
X\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
is also irreducible as an open subset of
X
. Using
[
EGA IV3
, 8.8.2, 8.10.5]
, there exists an index
\lambda_{0}\in\Lambda
, a proper
T_{\lambda_{0}}
-scheme
X_{\lambda_{0}}
, and an open subscheme
Y_{\lambda_{0}}
of
U_{\lambda_{0}}\times_{T_{\lambda_{0}}}X_{\lambda_{0}}
, such that
X=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})\times_{T_{\lambda_{0}}}X_{\lambda_{0}}
and that
Y=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(L)\times_{U_{\lambda_{0}}}Y_{\lambda_{0}}
. Let
\eta
denote the generic point of
X
,
\eta_{\lambda_{0}}
the image of
\eta
under the morphism
X\to X_{\lambda_{0}}
,
Z_{\lambda_{0}}
the scheme theoretic closure of
\eta_{\lambda_{0}}
in
X_{\lambda_{0}}
. Notice that
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})\times_{T_{\lambda_{0}}}Z_{\lambda_{0}}\to X
is a surjective finitely presented closed immersion. After replacing
X
by
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})\times_{T_{\lambda_{0}}}Z_{\lambda_{0}}
and replacing
X_{\lambda_{0}}
by
Z_{\lambda_{0}}
, we may assume that
X\to X_{\lambda_{0}}
is a dominant morphism of irreducible schemes. Since
T_{\lambda_{0}}
is irreducible and
L
is algebraically closed, the generic fibre of
f:X_{\lambda_{0}}\to T_{\lambda_{0}}
is geometrically irreducible. In particular, if
\xi_{\lambda_{0}}
(resp.
\eta_{\lambda_{0}}
) denotes the generic point of
T_{\lambda_{0}}
(resp.
X_{\lambda_{0}}
), then
\eta=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(L)\times_{\xi_{\lambda_{0}}}\eta_{\lambda_{0}}
(
[
EGA IV2
, 4.5.9]
). In the situation of (
iv
), we can moreover assume that
Y_{\lambda_{0}}=U_{\lambda_{0}}\times_{T_{\lambda_{0}}}X_{\lambda_{0}}
.

By
9.9
, there exists a commutative diagram of dominant open immersions of irreducible schemes,

\textstyle{(Y^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}\to X^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\beta^{\circ},\beta)}
\scriptstyle{(f^{\prime\circ},f^{\prime})}
\textstyle{(Y_{\lambda_{0}}\to X_{\lambda_{0}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f^{\circ},f)}
\textstyle{(U^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}\to T^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\alpha^{\circ},\alpha)}
\textstyle{(U_{\lambda_{0}}\to T_{\lambda_{0}})}

where
Y^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}\to X^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}
is Cartesian over
U^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}\times_{U_{\lambda_{0}}}Y_{\lambda_{0}}\to T^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}\times_{T_{\lambda_{0}}}X_{\lambda_{0}}
, and where
(X^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}},\mathscr{M}_{Y^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}\to X^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}})\to(T^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}},\mathscr{M}_{U^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}\to T^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}})
is a smooth and saturated morphism of fine, saturated and regular log schemes, and where
\alpha
and
\beta
are proper surjective and generically finite, and where
f^{\prime}
is projective surjective. We take a dominant morphism
\gamma^{\circ}:\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(L)\to U_{\lambda_{0}}^{\prime}
which lifts
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(L)\to U_{\lambda_{0}}
since
L
is algebraically closed and
\alpha
is generically finite, the morphism
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})\to T_{\lambda_{0}}
lifts to
\gamma:\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})\to T^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}
by the valuative criterion. We set
Y^{\prime}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(L)\times_{U^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}}Y^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}
and
X^{\prime}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})\times_{T^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}}X^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}
. It is clear that
Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}
is Cartesian over
Y\to X
by base change. Let
\xi^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}
(resp.
\eta^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}
) be the generic points of
T^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}
(resp.
X^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}
). Since the generic fibre of
f
is geometrically irreducible,
\xi^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}\times_{\xi_{\lambda_{0}}}\eta_{\lambda_{0}}
is a single point and
\eta^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}
maps to it (
[
EGA IV2
, 4.5.9]
). Since
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(L)\times_{\xi_{\lambda_{0}}}\eta_{\lambda_{0}}
is the generic point of
X
, we see that
X^{\prime}\to X
is proper surjective and of finite presentation.
It remains to construct
(Y^{\prime}_{\lambda}\to X^{\prime}_{\lambda})_{\lambda\geq\lambda_{1}}
.

After replacing
T^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}
by an affine open neighborhood of the image of the closed point of
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
, lemma
9.8
implies that there exists an index
\lambda_{1}\geq\lambda_{0}
such that the transition morphism
(U_{\lambda_{1}}\to T_{\lambda_{1}})\to(U_{\lambda_{0}}\to T_{\lambda_{0}})
factors through
(U^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}\to T^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}})
. For each index
\lambda\geq\lambda_{1}
, consider the fibred product in the category of fine and saturated log schemes

(9.11.5)

\displaystyle(X^{\prime}_{\lambda},\mathscr{M}_{Y^{\prime}_{\lambda}\to X^{\prime}_{\lambda}})=(T_{\lambda},\mathscr{M}_{U_{\lambda}\to T_{\lambda}})\times_{(T^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}},\mathscr{M}_{U^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}\to T^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}})}^{\mathrm{fs}}(X^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}},\mathscr{M}_{Y^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}\to X^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}}),

which is a fine, saturated and regular log scheme smooth and saturated over
(T_{\lambda},\mathscr{M}_{U_{\lambda}\to T_{\lambda}})
(
9.2
,
9.4
). Moreover, we have
Y^{\prime}_{\lambda}=U_{\lambda}\times_{U^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}}Y^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}
,
X^{\prime}_{\lambda}=T_{\lambda}\times_{T^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}}X^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}
, and in the situation of (
iv
),
Y^{\prime}_{\lambda}=U_{\lambda}\times_{T_{\lambda}}X^{\prime}_{\lambda}
by base change. Therefore,
(Y^{\prime}_{\lambda}\to X^{\prime}_{\lambda})_{\lambda\geq\lambda_{1}}
meets our requirements.
∎

10.
Faltings’ Main
p
-adic Comparison Theorem: the Absolute Case

Lemma 10.1
.

Let
Y
be a coherent scheme,
V
a finite étale
Y
-scheme. Then, there exists a finite étale surjective morphism
Y^{\prime}\to Y
such that
Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}V
is isomorphic to a finite disjoint union of
Y^{\prime}
.

Proof.

If
Y
is connected, let
\overline{y}
be a geometric point of
Y
,
\pi_{1}(Y,\overline{y})
the fundamental group of
Y
with base point
\overline{y}
. Then,
Y_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}
is equivalent to the category of finite
\pi_{1}(Y,\overline{y})
-sets so that the lemma holds (
[
Sta22
,
0BND
]
).

In general, for any connected component
Z
of
Y
, let
(Y_{\lambda})_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{Z}}
be the directed inverse system of all open and closed subschemes of
Y
which contain
Z
and whose transition morphisms are inclusions. Notice that
\lim_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{Z}}Y_{\lambda}
is a closed subscheme of
Y
with underlying topological space
Z
by
[
Sta22
,
04PL
]
and
[
EGA IV3
, 8.2.9]
. We endow
Z
with the closed subscheme structure of
\lim_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{Z}}Y_{\lambda}
. The first paragraph shows that there exists a finite étale surjective morphism
Z^{\prime}\to Z
such that
Z^{\prime}\times_{Y}V=\coprod_{i=1}^{r}Z^{\prime}
. Using
[
EGA IV3
, 8.8.2, 8.10.5]
and
[
EGA IV4
, 17.7.8]
, there exists an index
\lambda_{0}\in\Lambda_{Z}
, a finite étale surjective morphism
Y^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}\to Y_{\lambda_{0}}
and an isomorphism
Y^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}\times_{Y}V=\coprod_{i=1}^{r}Y^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}
. Notice that
Y^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}
is also finite étale over
Y
. Since
Z
is an arbitrary connected component of
Y
, the conclusion follows from the quasi-compactness of
Y
.
∎

Lemma 10.2
.

Let
Y
be a coherent scheme,
\rho:Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to Y_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}
the morphism of sites defined by the inclusion functor. Then, the functor
\rho^{-1}:\widetilde{Y_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}}\to\widetilde{Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
of the associated topoi induces an equivalence
\rho^{-1}:\mathbf{LocSys}(Y_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}})\to\mathbf{LocSys}(Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})
between the categories of finite locally constant abelian sheaves with quasi-inverse
\rho_{*}
.

Proof.

Since any finite locally constant sheaf on
Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
(resp.
Y_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}
) is representable by a finite étale
Y
-scheme by faithfully flat descent (cf.
[
Sta22
,
03RV
]
), the Yoneda embeddings induce a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{LocSys}(Y_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{-1}}
\textstyle{Y_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h^{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{Y_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{LocSys}(Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}}

where the horizontal arrows are fully faithful. In particular,
\rho^{-1}
is fully faithful. For a finite locally constant abelian sheaf
\mathbb{F}
on
Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
, let
V
be a finite étale
Y
-scheme representing
\mathbb{F}
and let
h_{V}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
(resp.
h_{V}^{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}
) be the representable sheaf of
V
on
Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
(resp.
Y_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}
). We have
\mathbb{F}=h_{V}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}=\rho^{-1}h_{V}^{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}
(
[
Sta22
,
04D3
]
). By
10.1
,
h_{V}^{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}
is finite locally constant. It is clear that the adjunction morphism
h_{V}^{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}\to\rho_{*}h_{V}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is an isomorphism, which shows that
h_{V}^{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}
is an abelian sheaf. Thus,
\rho^{-1}
is essentially surjective. Moreover, the argument also shows that
\rho_{*}
induces a functor
\rho_{*}:\mathbf{LocSys}(Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})\to\mathbf{LocSys}(Y_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}})
which is a quasi-inverse of
\rho^{-1}
.
∎

Proposition 10.3
.

With the notation in
7.8
, the functors between the categories of finite locally constant abelian sheaves

(10.3.1)

\displaystyle\mathbf{LocSys}(Y_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\beta^{-1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{LocSys}(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\psi^{-1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{LocSys}(Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})

are equivalences with quasi-inverses
\beta_{*}
and
\psi_{*}
respectively.

Proof.

Notice that for any finite locally constant abelian sheaf
\mathbb{G}
on
Y_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}
, the canonical morphism
\beta^{-1}\mathbb{G}\to\psi_{*}\rho^{-1}\mathbb{G}
, which is induced by the adjunction
\mathrm{id}\to\psi_{*}\psi^{-1}
and by the identity
\psi^{-1}\beta^{-1}=\rho^{-1}
, is an isomorphism by
10.2
and the proof of
[
AGT16
, VI.6.3.(iii)]
. For a finite locally constant abelian sheaf
\mathbb{F}
over
Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
, we write
\mathbb{F}=\rho^{-1}\mathbb{G}
by
10.2
. Then,
\mathbb{F}=\psi^{-1}\beta^{-1}\mathbb{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\psi^{-1}\psi_{*}\rho^{-1}\mathbb{G}=\psi^{-1}\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}
, whose inverse is the adjunction map
\psi^{-1}\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}\to\mathbb{F}
since the composition of
\psi^{-1}(\beta^{-1}\mathbb{G})\to\psi^{-1}(\psi_{*}\psi^{-1})(\beta^{-1}\mathbb{G})=(\psi^{-1}\psi_{*})\psi^{-1}(\beta^{-1}\mathbb{G})\to\psi^{-1}(\beta^{-1}\mathbb{G})
is the identity. On the other hand, for a finite locally constant abelian sheaf
\mathbb{L}
over
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
, we claim that
\mathbb{L}\to\psi_{*}\psi^{-1}\mathbb{L}
is an isomorphism. The problem is local on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. Thus, we may assume that
\mathbb{L}
is the constant sheaf with value
L
where
L
is a finite abelian group. Let
\underline{L}
be the constant sheaf with value
L
on
Y_{\mathrm{f\acute{e}t}}
. Then,
\mathbb{L}=\beta^{-1}\underline{L}
, and the isomorphism
\mathbb{L}=\beta^{-1}\underline{L}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\psi_{*}\rho^{-1}\underline{L}=\psi_{*}\psi^{-1}\mathbb{L}
coincides with the adjunction map
\mathbb{L}\to\psi_{*}\psi^{-1}\mathbb{L}
. Therefore,
\psi^{-1}:\mathbf{LocSys}(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})\to\mathbf{LocSys}(Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})
is an equivalence with quasi-inverse
\psi_{*}
. The conclusion follows from
10.2
.
∎

10.4
.

Let
f:(Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime})\to(Y\to X)
be a morphism of morphisms between coherent schemes over
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. The base change by
f
induces a commutative diagram of sites

\textstyle{Y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{\mathbf{E}}}
\textstyle{Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}

Let
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
be a finite locally constant abelian sheaf on
Y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. Remark that the sheaf
\overline{\mathscr{B}}
on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is flat over
\mathbb{Z}
. Consider the natural morphisms in the derived category
\mathbf{D}(\overline{\mathscr{B}}\textrm{-}\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}})
,

\textstyle{(\mathrm{R}\psi_{*}\mathrm{R}f_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime})\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}}
\textstyle{(\mathrm{R}f_{\mathbf{E}*}\psi^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime})\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}f_{\mathbf{E}*}(\psi^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\prime}),}

where
\alpha_{1}
is induced by the canonical morphism
\psi^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\to\mathrm{R}\psi^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
, and
\alpha_{2}
is the canonical morphism.

10.5
.

We keep the notation in
10.4
and assume that
X
is the spectrum of an absolutely integrally closed valuation ring
A
and that
Y
is a quasi-compact open subscheme of
X
. Applying the functor
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(Y\to X,-)
on (
10.4
), we obtain the natural morphisms in the derived category
\mathbf{D}(A\textrm{-}\mathbf{Mod})
by
7.9
,

\textstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma(Y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{F}^{\prime})\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}A}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\psi^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime})\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\psi^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\prime}).}

Definition 10.6

(
[
AG20
, 4.8.13, 5.7.3]
)
.

Under the assumptions in
10.4
(resp.
10.5
) and with the same notation, if
\alpha_{1}
is an isomorphism (for instance, if the canonical morphism
\psi^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\to\mathrm{R}\psi^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
is an isomorphism), then we call the canonical morphism

(10.6.1)

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}\circ\alpha_{1}^{-1}:(\mathrm{R}\psi_{*}\mathrm{R}f_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime})\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}f_{\mathbf{E}*}(\psi^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\prime})

(10.6.2)

(resp.

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}\circ\alpha_{1}^{-1}:\mathrm{R}\Gamma(Y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{F}^{\prime})\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}A\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\psi^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\prime})\textrm{)}

the
relative
(resp.
absolute
)
Faltings’ comparison morphism
associated to
f:(Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime})\to(Y\to X)
and
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
. In this case, we say that
Faltings’ comparison morphisms exist
.

Theorem 10.7

(
[
Ach17
, Cor.6.9]
, cf.
[
AG20
, 4.4.2]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{O}_{K}
be a strictly Henselian discrete valuation ring with fraction field
K
of characteristic
0
and residue field of characteristic
p
. We fix an algebraic closure
\overline{K}
of
K
. Let
X
be an
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-scheme of finite type,
\mathbb{F}
a finite locally constant abelian sheaf on
X_{\overline{K},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
,
\psi:X_{\overline{K},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{E}_{X_{\overline{K}}\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
the morphism of sites defined in
7.8
. Then, the canonical morphism
\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}\to\mathrm{R}\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}
is an isomorphism.

Corollary 10.8
.

Let
\mathcal{O}_{K}
be a strictly Henselian discrete valuation ring with fraction field
K
of characteristic
0
and residue field of characteristic
p
. We fix an algebraic closure
\overline{K}
of
K
. Let
X
be a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{K}}
-scheme,
Y=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\overline{K})\times_{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{\overline{K}})}X
,
\mathbb{F}
a finite locally constant abelian sheaf on
Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
,
\psi:Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
the morphism of sites defined in
7.8
. Then, the canonical morphism
\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}\to\mathrm{R}\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}
is an isomorphism.

We emphasize that we don’t need any finiteness condition of
X
over
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{K}}
in
10.8
. In fact, one can replace
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{K}}
by
\overline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
without loss of generality, where
\overline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
is the integral closure of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
in an algebraic closure of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. We keep working over
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{K}}
only for the continuation of our usage of notation.

Proof of
10.8
.

We take a directed inverse system
(X_{\lambda}\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\lambda}}))_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
of morphisms of finite type of schemes by Noetherian approximation, such that
K_{\lambda}
is a finite field extension of
K
and
\overline{K}=\bigcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda}K_{\lambda}
, and that the transition morphisms
X_{\lambda^{\prime}}\to X_{\lambda}
are affine and
X=\lim_{\lambda\in\Lambda}X_{\lambda}
(cf.
[
Sta22
,
09MV
]
). For each
\lambda\in\Lambda
, we set
Y_{\lambda}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\overline{K})\times_{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\lambda}})}X_{\lambda}
. Notice that
Y=\lim Y_{\lambda}
. Then, there exists an index
\lambda_{0}\in\Lambda
and a finite locally constant abelian sheaf
\mathbb{F}_{\lambda_{0}}
on
Y_{\lambda_{0},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
such that
\mathbb{F}
is the pullback of
\mathbb{F}_{\lambda_{0}}
by
Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to Y_{\lambda_{0},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
(cf.
[
Sta22
,
09YU
]
). Let
\mathbb{F}_{\lambda}
be the pullback of
\mathbb{F}_{\lambda_{0}}
by
Y_{\lambda,\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to Y_{\lambda_{0},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
for each
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}
. Notice that
\mathcal{O}_{K_{\lambda}}
also satisfies the conditions in
10.7
. Let
\psi_{\lambda}:Y_{\lambda,\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\lambda}\to X_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
be the morphism of sites defined in
7.8
,
\varphi_{\lambda}:\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\lambda}\to X_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
the morphism of sites defined by the transition morphism. Then, we have
\mathrm{R}^{q}\psi_{\lambda*}\mathbb{F}_{\lambda}=0
for each integer
q>0
by
10.7
, and moreover

(10.8.1)

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}^{q}\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}}\varphi_{\lambda}^{-1}\mathrm{R}^{q}\psi_{\lambda*}\mathbb{F}_{\lambda}=0

by
7.12
,
[
SGA 4

II

, VII.5.6]
and
[
SGA 4

II

, VI.8.7.3]
whose conditions are satisfied because each object in each concerned site is quasi-compact.
∎

Lemma 10.9
.

With the notation in
10.4
, let
\mathbb{F}
be a finite locally constant abelian sheaf on
Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. Then, the canonical morphism
f_{\mathbf{E}}^{-1}\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}\to\psi^{\prime}_{*}f_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}\mathbb{F}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The base change morphism
f_{\mathbf{E}}^{-1}\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}\to\psi^{\prime}_{*}f_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}\mathbb{F}
is the composition of the adjunction morphisms (
[
SGA 4

III

, XVII.2.1.3]
)

(10.9.1)

\displaystyle f_{\mathbf{E}}^{-1}\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}\to\psi^{\prime}_{*}\psi^{\prime-1}(f_{\mathbf{E}}^{-1}\psi_{*}\mathbb{F})=\psi^{\prime}_{*}f_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}(\psi^{-1}\psi_{*}\mathbb{F})\to\psi^{\prime}_{*}f_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}\mathbb{F}

which are both isomorphisms by
10.3
.
∎

10.10
.

Let
K
be a complete discrete valuation field of characteristic
0
with valuation ring
\mathcal{O}_{K}
whose residue field
k
is algebraically closed (a condition required by
[
AG20
, 4.1.3, 5.1.3]
) of characteristic
p>0
,
\overline{K}
an algebraic closure of
K
,
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{K}}
the integral closure of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
in
\overline{K}
,
\eta=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K)
,
\overline{\eta}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\overline{K})
,
S=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
,
\overline{S}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{\overline{K}})
,
s=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(k)
. Remark that
\overline{K}
is a pre-perfectoid field with valuation ring
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{K}}
so we are also in the situation
8.7
.

10.11
.

With the notation in
10.10
, let
X
be an
S
-scheme,
Y
an open subscheme of the generic fibre
X_{\eta}
. We simply denote by
\mathscr{M}_{X}
the compactifying log structure
\mathscr{M}_{X_{\eta}\to X}
(
9.3
). Following
[
AGT16
, III.4.7]
, we say that
Y\to X
is
adequate
over
\eta\to S
if the following conditions are satisfied:

(i)

X
is of finite type over
S
;

(ii)

Any point of the special fibre
X_{s}
admits an étale neighborhood
U
such that
U_{\eta}\to\eta
is smooth and that
U_{\eta}\setminus Y
is the support of a strict normal crossings divisor on
U_{\eta}
;

(iii)

(X,\mathscr{M}_{Y\to X})
is a fine log scheme and the structure morphism
(X,\mathscr{M}_{Y\to X})\to(S,\mathscr{M}_{S})
is smooth and saturated.

In this case,
(X,\mathscr{M}_{Y\to X})\to(S,\mathscr{M}_{S})
is adequate in the sense of
[
AGT16
, III.4.7]
. We remark that for any adequate
(S,\mathscr{M}_{S})
-log scheme
(X,\mathscr{M})
,
X^{\mathrm{tr}}\to X
is adequate over
\eta\to S
and
(X,\mathscr{M})=(X,\mathscr{M}_{X^{\mathrm{tr}}\to X})
(cf.
9.4
,
9.5
).
Note that if
Y\to X
is semi-stable over
\eta\to S
then it is adequate (cf.
9.5
).

10.12
.

We recall the statement of Faltings’ main
p
-adic comparison theorem following Abbes-Gros
[
AG20
]
. We take the notation and assumptions in
10.10
. Firstly, recall that for any adequate open immersion of schemes
X^{\circ}\to X
over
\eta\to S
and any finite locally constant abelian sheaf
\mathbb{F}
on
X^{\circ}_{\overline{\eta},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
, the canonical morphism
\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}\to\mathrm{R}\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}
is an isomorphism, where
\psi:X^{\circ}_{\overline{\eta},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{E}_{X_{\overline{\eta}}^{\circ}\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is the morphism of sites defined in
7.8
(
[
AG20
, 4.4.2]
).

Let
(X^{\prime\triangleright}\to X^{\prime})\to(X^{\circ}\to X)
be a morphism of adequate open immersions of schemes over
\eta\to S
such that
X^{\prime}\to X
is projective and that the induced morphism
(X^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{X^{\prime\triangleright}\to X^{\prime}})\to(X,\mathscr{M}_{X^{\circ}\to X})
is smooth and saturated. Let
Y^{\prime}=\overline{\eta}\times_{\eta}X^{\prime\triangleright}
,
Y=\overline{\eta}\times_{\eta}X^{\circ}
,
f:(Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime})\to(Y\to X)
the natural morphism,
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
a finite locally constant abelian sheaf on
Y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. By the first paragraph, we have the relative Faltings’ comparison morphism associated to
f
and
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
(
10.6.1
),

(10.12.1)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{R}\psi_{*}\mathrm{R}f_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime})\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}f_{\mathbf{E}*}(\psi^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\prime}).

Remark that under our assumption, the sheaf
\mathrm{R}^{q}f_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
on
Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is finite locally constant for each integer
q
(
[
AG20
, 2.2.25]
).

Theorem 10.13

(
[
Fal02
, Thm.6, page 266]
,
[
AG20
, 5.7.3]
)
.

Under the assumptions in
10.12
and with the same notation, the relative Faltings’ comparison morphism associated to
f
and
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
is an almost isomorphism in the derived category
\mathbf{D}(\mathcal{O}_{\overline{K}}\textrm{-}\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}})

(
5.7
)
, and it induces an almost isomorphism

(10.13.1)

\displaystyle(\psi_{*}\mathrm{R}^{q}f_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}^{q}f_{\mathbf{E}*}(\psi^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\prime})

of
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{K}}
-modules for each integer
q
.

Proposition 10.14
.

With the natation in
10.10
, let
A
be an absolutely integrally closed valuation ring of height
1
extension of
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{K}}
,
X
a proper
A
-scheme of finite presentation,
Y=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A[1/p])\times_{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)}X
,
\mathbb{F}
a finite locally constant abelian sheaf on
Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. Then, there exists a proper surjective morphism
X^{\prime}\to X
of finite presentation such that the relative and absolute Faltings’ comparison morphisms associated to
f^{\prime}:(Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime})\to(\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A[1/p])\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A))
and
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
(which exist by
10.8
) are almost isomorphisms, where
Y^{\prime}=Y\times_{X}X^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
is the pullback of
\mathbb{F}
on
Y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
.

Proof.

Since the underlying topological space of
X
is Noetherian by
9.10
, each irreducible component
Z
of
X
admits a closed subscheme structure such that
Z\to X
is of finite presentation (
[
Sta22
,
01PH
]
). After replacing
X
by the disjoint union of its irreducible components, we may assume that
X
is irreducible. If
Y
is empty, then we take
X^{\prime}=X
and thus the relative (resp. absolute) Faltings’ comparison morphism associated to
f^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
is an isomorphism between zero objects. If
Y
is not empty, then we are in the situation of
9.11
.(
iv
) by taking
\mathcal{O}_{L}=A
. With the notation in
9.11
, we check that the morphism
X^{\prime}\to X
meets our requirements. We set
\eta_{\lambda}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K_{\lambda})
,
S_{\lambda}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\lambda}})
,
T_{\lambda,\overline{\eta_{\lambda}}}=\overline{\eta}\times_{\eta_{\lambda}}U_{\lambda}
,
X^{\prime}_{\lambda,\overline{\eta_{\lambda}}}=\overline{\eta}\times_{\eta_{\lambda}}Y^{\prime}_{\lambda}
, and denote by
f^{\prime}_{\lambda}:(X^{\prime}_{\lambda,\overline{\eta_{\lambda}}}\to X^{\prime}_{\lambda})\to(T_{\lambda,\overline{\eta_{\lambda}}}\to T_{\lambda})
the natural morphism. We obtain a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\lambda,\mathbf{E}}}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}_{\mathbf{E}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{X^{\prime}_{\lambda,\overline{\eta_{\lambda}}}\to X^{\prime}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}_{\lambda,\mathbf{E}}}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\lambda,\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}_{\lambda,\overline{\eta_{\lambda}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}_{\lambda,\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{\lambda}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A[1/p])_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h_{\lambda,\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{T_{\lambda,\overline{\eta_{\lambda}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{\lambda}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A[1/p])\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h_{\lambda,\mathbf{E}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{T_{\lambda,\overline{\eta_{\lambda}}}\to T_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}

Firstly notice that the site
Y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
(resp.
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A[1/p])_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
) is the limit of the sites
X^{\prime}_{\lambda,\overline{\eta_{\lambda}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
(resp.
T_{\lambda,\overline{\eta_{\lambda}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
) and the site
\mathbf{E}_{Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
(resp.
\mathbf{E}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A[1/p])\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
) is the limit of the sites
\mathbf{E}_{X^{\prime}_{\lambda,\overline{\eta_{\lambda}}}\to X^{\prime}_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
(resp.
\mathbf{E}_{T_{\lambda,\overline{\eta_{\lambda}}}\to T_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
) (
[
SGA 4

II

, VII.5.6]
and
7.12
). There exists an index
\lambda_{0}\in\Lambda
and a finite locally constant abelian sheaf
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}
on
X^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0},\overline{\eta_{\lambda_{0}}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
such that
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
is the pullback of
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}
by
Y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to X^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0},\overline{\eta_{\lambda_{0}}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
(cf.
[
Sta22
,
09YU
]
). Let
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
be the pullback of
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}
by
X^{\prime}_{\lambda,\overline{\eta_{\lambda}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to X^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0},\overline{\eta_{\lambda_{0}}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
for each
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}
. We also have
\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\prime}=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}g_{\lambda,\mathbf{E}}^{-1}\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\prime}
(resp.
\overline{\mathscr{B}}=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}h_{\lambda,\mathbf{E}}^{-1}\overline{\mathscr{B}}
) by
7.12
. According to
[
SGA 4

II

, VI.8.7.3]
, whose conditions are satisfied because each object in each concerned site is quasi-compact, there are canonical isomorphisms for each integer
q
,

(10.14.9)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{R}^{q}(\psi\circ f^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}h_{\lambda,\mathbf{E}}^{-1}((\mathrm{R}^{q}(\psi_{\lambda}\circ f^{\prime}_{\lambda,\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}_{\lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}),

(10.14.10)

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}^{q}f^{\prime}_{\mathbf{E}*}(\psi^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}h_{\lambda,\mathbf{E}}^{-1}\mathrm{R}^{q}f^{\prime}_{\lambda,\mathbf{E}*}(\psi^{\prime}_{\lambda*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}_{\lambda}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\prime}).

On the other hand,
(X^{\prime}_{\lambda},\mathscr{M}_{X^{\prime}_{\lambda}})\to(T_{\lambda},\mathscr{M}_{T_{\lambda}})
is a smooth and saturated morphism of adequate
(S_{\lambda},\mathscr{M}_{S_{\lambda}})
-log schemes with
X^{\prime}_{\lambda}\to T_{\lambda}
projective for each
\lambda\in\Lambda
by construction. Thus, we are in the situation of
10.13
, which implies that the relative Faltings’ comparison morphism associated to
f^{\prime}_{\lambda}
and
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
,

(10.14.11)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{R}^{q}(\psi_{\lambda}\circ f^{\prime}_{\lambda,\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}_{\lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}^{q}f^{\prime}_{\lambda,\mathbf{E}*}(\psi^{\prime}_{\lambda*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}_{\lambda}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\prime})

is an almost isomorphism for each
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}
. Combining with (
10.14.9
) and (
10.14.10
), we see that the relative Faltings’ comparison morphism associated to
f^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
,

(10.14.12)

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\psi_{*}(\mathrm{R}f^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}f^{\prime}_{\mathbf{E}*}(\psi^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\prime}),

is an almost isomorphism (and thus so is the absolute one).
∎

Corollary 10.15
.

Under the assumptions in
10.14
and with the same notation, there exists a proper hypercovering
X_{\bullet}\to X
of coherent schemes
(
[
Sta22
,
0DHI
]
)
such that for each degree
n
, the relative and absolute Faltings’ comparison morphisms associated to
f_{n}:(Y_{n}\to X_{n})\to(\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A[1/p])\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A))
and
\mathbb{F}_{n}
(which exist by
10.8
) are almost isomorphisms, where
Y_{n}=Y\times_{X}X_{n}
and
\mathbb{F}_{n}
is the pullback of
\mathbb{F}
by the natural morphism
Y_{n,\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. In particular,
Y_{\bullet}\to Y
is a proper hypercovering and
X_{\bullet}^{Y_{\bullet}}\to X^{Y}
is a hypercovering in
\mathbf{I}_{\overline{\eta}\to\overline{S}}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be the category of proper
A
-schemes of finite presentation endowed with the pretopology formed by families of morphisms
\{f_{i}:X_{i}\to X\}_{i\in I}
with
I
finite and
X=\bigcup_{i\in I}f_{i}(X_{i})
. Consider the functor
u^{+}:\mathscr{C}\to\mathbf{I}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A[1/p])\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)}
sending
X
to
X^{Y}
where
Y=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A[1/p])\times_{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)}X
. It is well-defined by
3.19
.(
4
) and commutes with fibred products by
3.21
and continuous by
3.15
. Lemma
10.14
allows us to take a hypercovering
X_{\bullet}\to X
in
\mathscr{C}
meeting our requirement by
[
Sta22
,
094K
and
0DB1
]
. We see that
Y_{\bullet}\to Y
is a proper hypercovering and that
X_{\bullet}^{Y_{\bullet}}\to X^{Y}
is a hypercovering in
\mathbf{I}_{\overline{\eta}\to\overline{S}}
by the properties of
u^{+}
(
[
Sta22
,
0DAY
]
).
∎

Lemma 10.16
.

Let
\overline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
be the integral closure of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
in an algebraic closure of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
,
A
a
\overline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-algebra which is an absolutely integrally closed valuation ring,
X
a proper
A
-scheme of finite presentation,
Y=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A[1/p])\times_{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)}X
,
\mathbb{F}
a finite locally constant abelian sheaf on
Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. Let
A^{\prime}=((A/\cap_{n>0}p^{n}A)_{\sqrt{pA}})^{\wedge}
(
p
-adic completion),
X^{\prime}=X_{A^{\prime}}
,
Y^{\prime}=Y_{A^{\prime}}
,
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
the pullback of
\mathbb{F}
on
Y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. Then, the following statements are equivalent:

(1)

The absolute Faltings’ comparison morphism associated to
f:(Y\to X)\to(\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A[1/p])\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A))
and
\mathbb{F}
(which exists by
10.8
) is an almost isomorphism.

(2)

The absolute Faltings’ comparison morphism associated to
f^{\prime}:(Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime})\to(\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A^{\prime}[1/p])\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A^{\prime}))
and
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
(which exists by
10.8
) is an almost isomorphism.

Proof.

If
p
is zero (resp. invertible) in
A
, then the absolute Faltings’ comparison morphisms are both isomorphisms between zero objects, since
Y
and
Y^{\prime}
are empty (resp. the abelian sheaves
\mathbb{F}
and
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
are zero after inverting
p
). Thus, we may assume that
p
is a nonzero element of the maximal ideal of
A
. Notice that
\cap_{n>0}p^{n}A
is the maximal prime ideal of
A
not containing
p
and that
\sqrt{pA}
is the minimal prime ideal of
A
containing
p
(
2.1
). Thus,
(A/\cap_{n>0}p^{n}A)_{\sqrt{pA}}
is an absolutely integrally closed valuation ring of height
1
extension of
\overline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
(
2.1
) and thus so is its
p
-adic completion
A^{\prime}
.

We denote by
u:(Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime})\to(Y\to X)
the natural morphism. We have
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}=u_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}\mathbb{F}
. The natural morphisms in (
10.5
) induce a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma(Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{F})\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\psi_{*}\mathbb{F})\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{3}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma(Y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{F}^{\prime})\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}A^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\psi^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime})\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}A^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{1}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{2}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\psi^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\prime})}

where
\gamma_{1}
is induced by the canonical morphism
\mathbb{F}\to\mathrm{R}u_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}u_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}\mathbb{F}
, and
\gamma_{2}
(resp.
\gamma_{3}
) is induced by the composition of
\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}\to\mathrm{R}u_{\mathbf{E}*}u_{\mathbf{E}}^{-1}\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}\to\mathrm{R}u_{\mathbf{E}*}\psi^{\prime}_{*}u_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}\mathbb{F}
(resp. and by the canonical morphism
\overline{\mathscr{B}}\to\mathrm{R}u_{\mathbf{E}*}\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\prime}
). Since
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{1}^{\prime}
are isomorphisms by
10.8
, it suffices to show that
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{3}
are almost isomorphisms.

Since
A/\cap_{n>0}p^{n}A\to(A/\cap_{n>0}p^{n}A)_{\sqrt{pA}}
is injective whose cokernel is killed by
\sqrt{pA}
(
4.7
), the morphism
A\to A^{\prime}
induces an almost isomorphism
A/p^{n}A\to A^{\prime}/p^{n}A^{\prime}
for each
n
. Then, for any torsion abelian group
M
, the natural morphism
M\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}A\to M\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}A^{\prime}
is an almost isomorphism. Therefore,
\gamma_{1}
is an almost isomorphism by the proper base change theorem over the strictly Henselian local ring
A[1/p]
(
[
SGA 4

III

, XII 5.5, 5.4]
). For
\gamma_{3}
, it suffices to show that the canonical morphism
\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}\otimes\overline{\mathscr{B}}\to\mathrm{R}u_{\mathbf{E}*}(\psi^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\otimes\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\prime})
is an almost isomorphism. The problem is local on
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
, thus we may assume that
\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}
is the constant sheaf with value
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
by
10.3
. Then,
\psi^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
is also the constant sheaf with value
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
by
10.9
. Let
V\to U
be an object of
\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
such that
U^{V}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)
is the spectrum of an
\overline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-algebra
R
which is almost pre-perfectoid. Since the almost isomorphisms
R/p^{n}\to(R\otimes_{A}A^{\prime})/p^{n}
(
n\geq 1
) induces an almost isomorphism of the
p
-adic completions
\widehat{R}\to R\widehat{\otimes}_{A}A^{\prime}
, the
\overline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-algebra
R\otimes_{A}A^{\prime}
is still almost pre-perfectoid (
5.19
). The pullback of
V\to U
in
\mathbf{E}_{Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
is the object
V_{A^{\prime}}\to U_{A^{\prime}}
and
U_{A^{\prime}}^{V_{A^{\prime}}}
is the spectrum of the integral closure
R^{\prime}
of
R\otimes_{A}A^{\prime}
in
R\otimes_{A}A^{\prime}[1/p]
. Since
R\otimes_{A}A^{\prime}
is almost pre-perfectoid,
R^{\prime}
is also almost pre-perfectoid and the morphism
(R\otimes_{A}A^{\prime})/p^{n}\to R^{\prime}/p^{n}
is an almost isomorphism by
5.30
. Therefore, the morphism
\overline{\mathscr{B}}/p^{n}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\to\mathrm{R}u_{\mathbf{E}*}(\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\prime}/p^{n}\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\prime})
is an almost isomorphism by
7.32
,
8.11
and
8.12
.
∎

Theorem 10.17
.

Let
\overline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
be the integral closure of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
in an algebraic closure of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
,
A
a
\overline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-algebra which is an absolutely integrally closed valuation ring,
X
a proper
A
-scheme of finite presentation,
Y=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A[1/p])\times_{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)}X
,
\mathbb{F}
a finite locally constant abelian sheaf on
Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. Then, the absolute Faltings’ comparison morphism associated to
f:(Y\to X)\to(\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A[1/p])\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A))
and
\mathbb{F}
(
10.6.2
) (which exists by
10.8
),

(10.17.1)

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\Gamma(Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{F})\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}A\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}),

is an almost isomorphism in
\mathbf{D}(\overline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\textrm{-}\mathbf{Mod})

(
5.7
)
.

Proof.

Let
K
be the
p
-adic completion of the maximal unramified extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. By
10.16
, we may assume that
A
is a valuation ring of height
1
