extension of
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{K}}
. Let
X_{\bullet}\to X
be the proper hypercovering of coherent schemes constructed in
10.15
. For each degree
n
the canonical morphisms (
10.6.2
)

(10.17.2)

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\Gamma(Y_{n,\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{F}_{n})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}A\longleftarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{Y_{n}\to X_{n}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\psi_{n*}\mathbb{F}_{n})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}A\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{Y_{n}\to X_{n}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\psi_{n*}\mathbb{F}_{n}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}})

are an isomorphism and an almost isomorphism, where
\mathbb{F}_{n}
is the pullback of
\mathbb{F}
by the natural morphism
Y_{n,\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. Consider the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma(Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{F})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\psi_{*}\mathbb{F})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma(Y_{\bullet,\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{F}_{\bullet})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}A}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\bullet}\to X_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\psi_{\bullet*}\mathbb{F}_{\bullet})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{1\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{2\bullet}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\bullet}\to X_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\psi_{\bullet*}\mathbb{F}_{\bullet}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}_{\bullet})}

where
\mathbb{F}_{\bullet}=(\mathbb{F}_{n})_{[n]\in\mathrm{Ob}(\Delta)}
with the notation in
6.5
. By the functorial spectral sequence of simplicial sites (
[
Sta22
,
09WJ
]
), we deduce from (
10.17.2
) that
\alpha_{1\bullet}
is an isomorphism and
\alpha_{2\bullet}
is an almost isomorphism. Since
\alpha_{1}
is an isomorphism by
10.8
, it remains to show that the left vertical arrow is an isomorphism and the right vertical arrow is an almost isomorphism.

We denote by
a:\mathbf{E}_{Y_{\bullet}\to X_{\bullet}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
the augmentation of simplicial site and by
a_{n}:\mathbf{E}_{Y_{n}\to X_{n}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
the natural morphism of sites. Notice that
a^{-1}\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}=(a_{n}^{-1}\psi_{*}\mathbb{F})_{[n]\in\mathrm{Ob}(\Delta)}=(\psi_{n*}\mathbb{F}_{n})_{[n]\in\mathrm{Ob}(\Delta)}=\psi_{\bullet*}\mathbb{F}_{\bullet}
by
10.9
(
[
Sta22
,
0D70
]
).
Since
X_{\bullet}^{Y_{\bullet}}\to X^{Y}
forms a hypercovering in
\mathbf{I}_{\overline{\eta}\to\overline{S}}
, the right vertical arrow is an almost isomorphism by
10.3
and
8.18
. Finally, the left vertical arrow is an isomorphism by the cohomological descent for étale cohomology
[
Sta22
,
0DHL
]
.
∎

-adic Comparison Theorem: the Relative Case for More General Coefficients

.

Let

\textstyle{(\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{/Y})_{\mathrm{v}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Lemma 11.2
.

With the notation in

, for any finite locally constant abelian sheaf
\mathbb{F}
on

The base change morphism

is the composition of the adjunction morphisms (
[

III

, XVII.2.1.3]
)

(11.2.1)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-1}\psi_{*}\mathbb{F}\to\Psi_{*}\Psi^{-1}(\varepsilon^{-1}\psi_{*}\mathbb{F})=\Psi_{*}a^{-1}(\psi^{-1}\psi_{*}\mathbb{F})\to\Psi_{*}a^{-1}\mathbb{F}

which are both isomorphisms by

.(

) and

.

Lemma 11.5
.

With the notation in
11.3
and
11.4
, let
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
be a finite locally constant abelian sheaf on

\widetilde{X}
be an object of
\mathbf{I}_{Y\to X^{Y}}
,

(1)

(3)

The canonical morphism

is compatible with the canonical morphisms
H^{q}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\widetilde{Y^{\prime}},\widetilde{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}R\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{1}}}{{\longleftarrow}}H^{q}(\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{Y^{\prime}}\to\widetilde{X^{\prime}}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\widetilde{\psi^{\prime}}_{*}\widetilde{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}R\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{q}(\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{Y^{\prime}}\to\widetilde{X^{\prime}}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\widetilde{\psi^{\prime}}_{*}\widetilde{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\prime})

(1) It follows from the canonical isomorphisms

(3) Consider the following diagram

Lemma 11.7
.

With the notation in
11.3
, let
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
be a finite locally constant abelian sheaf on

is proper of finite presentation. Then, the canonical morphism

(11.7.1)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{R}f_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}\mathscr{O}\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}f_{\mathbf{I}*}(\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{O}^{\prime})

Following

, consider the following presheaves on
\mathbf{I}_{Y\to X^{Y}}
for each integer

:

(11.7.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{1}^{q}

\displaystyle:\widetilde{X}\longmapsto H^{q}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\widetilde{Y^{\prime}},\widetilde{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}(\widetilde{Y}\to\widetilde{X}),

(11.7.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{2}^{q}

\displaystyle:\widetilde{X}\longmapsto H^{q}(\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{Y^{\prime}}\to\widetilde{X^{\prime}}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\widetilde{\psi^{\prime}}_{*}\widetilde{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}(\widetilde{Y}\to\widetilde{X}),

(11.7.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{3}^{q}

\displaystyle:\widetilde{X}\longmapsto H^{q}(\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{Y^{\prime}}\to\widetilde{X^{\prime}}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\widetilde{\psi^{\prime}}_{*}\widetilde{\mathbb{F}^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\prime}),

They satisfy the limit-preserving condition
3.25
.(
ii
) by
7.12
,
[
SGA 4

II

, VII.5.6]
and
[
SGA 4

II

, VI 8.5.9, 8.7.3]
. Moreover, if
\widetilde{X}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)
where
A
is an absolutely integrally closed valuation ring with
p
nonzero in
A
, then the canonical morphisms

(11.7.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{1}^{q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A))\leftarrow\mathcal{H}_{2}^{q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A))\to\mathcal{H}_{3}^{q}(\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A))

are an isomorphism and an almost isomorphism by
10.17
. Thus, the canonical morphisms
\mathcal{H}_{1}^{q}\leftarrow\mathcal{H}_{2}^{q}\to\mathcal{H}_{3}^{q}
induce an isomorphism and an almost isomorphism of their sheafifications by
3.25
. The conclusion follows from
11.5
.
∎

Lemma 11.8
.

Let
Y\to X
be an open immersion of coherent schemes,
Y^{\prime}\to Y
a finite morphism of finite presentation. Then, there exists a finite morphism
X^{\prime}\to X
of finite presentation whose base change by
Y\to X
is
Y^{\prime}\to Y
.

Proof.

Firstly, assume that
X
is Noetherian. We have
Y^{\prime}=Y\times_{X}X^{Y}
by
3.19
.(
4
). We write
X^{Y}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}_{X}(\mathcal{A})
where
\mathcal{A}
is an integral quasi-coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-algebra on
X
, and we write
\mathcal{A}
as a filtered colimit of its finite quasi-coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-subalgebras
\mathcal{A}=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}
(
[
Sta22
,
0817
]
). Let
\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}
be the restriction of
\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}
to
Y
. Then,
\mathcal{B}=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}
is a filtered colimit of finite quasi-coherent
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-algebras with injective transition morphisms. Since
Y^{\prime}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}_{Y}(\mathcal{B})
is finite over
Y
, there exists an index
\alpha_{0}
such that
Y^{\prime}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}_{Y}(\mathcal{B}_{\alpha_{0}})
. Therefore,
X^{\prime}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}_{X}(\mathcal{A}_{\alpha_{0}})
meets our requirements.

In general, we write
X
as a cofiltered limit of coherent schemes of finite type over
\mathbb{Z}
with affine transition morphisms
X=\lim_{\lambda\in\Lambda}X_{\lambda}
(
[
Sta22
,
01ZA
]
). Since
Y\to X
is an open immersion of finite presentation, using
[
EGA IV3
, 8.8.2, 8.10.5]
there exists an index
\lambda_{0}\in\Lambda
, an open immersion
Y_{\lambda_{0}}\to X_{\lambda_{0}}
and a finite morphism
Y^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}\to Y_{\lambda_{0}}
such that the base change of the morphisms
Y^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}\to Y_{\lambda_{0}}\to X_{\lambda_{0}}
by
X\to X_{\lambda_{0}}
are the morphisms
Y^{\prime}\to Y\to X
. By the first paragraph, there exists a finite morphism
X^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}\to X_{\lambda_{0}}
of finite presentation such that
Y^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}=Y_{\lambda_{0}}\times_{X_{\lambda_{0}}}X^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}
. We see that the base change
X^{\prime}\to X
of
X^{\prime}_{\lambda_{0}}\to X_{\lambda_{0}}
by
X\to X_{\lambda_{0}}
meets our requirements.
∎

Lemma 11.9
.

With the notation in
11.3
, let
g:Y^{\prime\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
be a finite morphism of finite presentation,
\mathbb{F}^{\prime\prime}
a finite locally constant abelian sheaf on
Y^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
and we set
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}=\Psi^{\prime}_{*}a^{\prime-1}(g_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime\prime})
. Assume that
X^{\prime}\to X
is proper of finite presentation. Then, the canonical morphism

(11.9.1)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{R}f_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}\mathscr{O}\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}f_{\mathbf{I}*}(\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{O}^{\prime})

is an almost isomorphism.

Proof.

There exists a Cartesian morphism
g:(Y^{\prime\prime}\to X^{\prime\prime})\to(Y^{\prime}\to X^{Y}\times_{X}X^{\prime})
of open immersions of coherent schemes such that
X^{\prime\prime}\to X^{Y}\times_{X}X^{\prime}
is finite and of finite presentation by
11.8
. Consider the diagram (
11.3
) associated to
g
:

\textstyle{Y^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
\textstyle{(\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{/Y^{\prime\prime}})_{\mathrm{v}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\Psi^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathrm{v}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{I}_{Y^{\prime\prime}\to X^{\prime\prime Y^{\prime\prime}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathbf{I}}}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
\textstyle{(\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{/Y^{\prime}})_{\mathrm{v}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\Psi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{I}_{Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime Y^{\prime}}}}

We set
\mathscr{G}^{\prime\prime}=\Psi^{\prime\prime}_{*}a^{\prime\prime-1}\mathbb{F}^{\prime\prime}
. The base change morphism
a^{\prime-1}g_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}\to g_{\mathrm{v}*}a^{\prime\prime-1}
induces a canonical isomorphism
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}g_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{G}^{\prime\prime}
by
3.10
. Moreover, the canonical morphism
g_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{G}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathrm{R}g_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{G}^{\prime\prime}
is an isomorphism by
11.5
.(
1
) and
3.25
, since
g:Y^{\prime\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
is finite (
[
SGA 4

II

, VIII.5.6]
). By applying
11.7
to
g
and
\mathbb{F}^{\prime\prime}
, the canonical morphism

(11.9.6)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{R}g_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{G}^{\prime\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}\mathscr{O}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}g_{\mathbf{I}*}(\mathscr{G}^{\prime\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{O}^{\prime\prime})

is an almost isomorphism. Let
h
be the composition of
(Y^{\prime\prime}\to X^{\prime\prime})\to(Y^{\prime}\to X^{Y}\times_{X}X^{\prime})\to(Y\to X^{Y})
. Note that
X^{\prime\prime}\to X^{Y}
is also proper of finite presentation. By applying
11.7
to
h
and
\mathbb{F}^{\prime\prime}
, the canonical morphism

(11.9.7)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{R}h_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{G}^{\prime\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}\mathscr{O}\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}h_{\mathbf{I}*}(\mathscr{G}^{\prime\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{O}^{\prime\prime})

is an almost isomorphism. It is clear that

. The conclusion follows from the canonical isomorphism
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\to\mathrm{R}g_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{G}^{\prime\prime}
and the canonical almost isomorphisms (
11.9.6
) and (
11.9.7
).
∎

Lemma 11.10
.

With the notation in
11.3
, let
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
be a constructible abelian sheaf on
Y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
and we set
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}=\Psi^{\prime}_{*}a^{\prime-1}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
. Assume that
X^{\prime}\to X
is proper of finite presentation. Then, the canonical morphism

(11.10.1)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{R}f_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}\mathscr{O}\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}f_{\mathbf{I}*}(\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{O}^{\prime})

is an almost isomorphism.

Proof.

We prove by induction on an integer
q
that the canonical morphism
(\mathrm{R}^{q}f_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{O}\to\mathrm{R}^{q}f_{\mathbf{I}*}(\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{O}^{\prime})
is an almost isomorphism. It holds trivially for each
q\leq-1
. Notice that there exists a finite morphism
g:Y^{\prime\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
of finite presentation, a finite locally constant abelian sheaf
\mathbb{F}^{\prime\prime}
on

and an injective morphism
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\to g_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime\prime}
by
[
Sta22
,
09Z7
]
(cf.
[
SGA 4

III

, IX.2.14]
). Let
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
be the quotient of
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\to g_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime\prime}
, which is also a constructible abelian sheaf on
Y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
since
g_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime\prime}
is so (
[
Sta22
,
095R
,
03RZ
]
). The exact sequence
0\to\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\to g_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\to 0
induces an exact sequence by
3.27
.(
1
),

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Psi^{\prime}_{*}a^{\prime-1}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Psi^{\prime}_{*}a^{\prime-1}(g_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Psi^{\prime}_{*}a^{\prime-1}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

We set
\mathscr{H}^{\prime}=\Psi^{\prime}_{*}a^{\prime-1}(g_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime\prime})
and
\mathscr{G}^{\prime}=\Psi^{\prime}_{*}a^{\prime-1}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Then, we obtain a morphism of long exact sequences

\textstyle{(\mathrm{R}^{q-1}f_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{H}^{\prime})\otimes\mathscr{O}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{1}}
\textstyle{(\mathrm{R}^{q-1}f_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{G}^{\prime})\otimes\mathscr{O}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{2}}
\textstyle{(\mathrm{R}^{q}f_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\otimes\mathscr{O}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{3}}
\textstyle{(\mathrm{R}^{q}f_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{H}^{\prime})\otimes\mathscr{O}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{4}}
\textstyle{(\mathrm{R}^{q}f_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{G}^{\prime})\otimes\mathscr{O}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{5}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}^{q-1}f_{\mathbf{I}*}(\mathscr{H}^{\prime}\otimes\mathscr{O}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}^{q-1}f_{\mathbf{I}*}(\mathscr{G}^{\prime}\otimes\mathscr{O}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Notice that
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
are almost isomorphisms by induction, and that
\gamma_{4}
is an almost isomorphism by

. Thus, applying the 5-lemma (
[
Sta22
,
05QA
]
) in the abelian category of almost

-modules over

, we see that
\gamma_{3}
is almost injective. Since
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is an arbitrary constructible abelian sheaf, the morphism
\gamma_{5}
is also almost injective. Thus,
\gamma_{3}
is an almost isomorphism.
∎

Theorem 11.11
.

With the notation in
11.3
, let
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
be a torsion abelian sheaf on
Y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
and we set
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}=\Psi^{\prime}_{*}a^{\prime-1}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
. Assume that
X^{\prime}\to X
is proper of finite presentation. Then, the canonical morphism

(11.11.1)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{R}f_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}\mathscr{O}\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}f_{\mathbf{I}*}(\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{O}^{\prime})

is an almost isomorphism in the derived category
\mathbf{D}(\overline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\textrm{-}\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathbf{I}_{Y\to X^{Y}}})

(
5.7
)
.

Proof.

We write
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
as a filtered colimit of constructible abelian sheaves
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
(
[
Sta22
,
03SA
]
, cf.
[
SGA 4

III

, IX.2.7.2]
). We set
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}_{\lambda}=\Psi^{\prime}_{*}a^{\prime-1}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}
. We have
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\mathscr{F}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
by
[
SGA 4

II

, VI.5.1]
whose conditions are satisfied since each object in each concerned site is quasi-compact. Moreover, for each integer
q
, we have

(11.11.2)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{R}^{q}f_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{O}=

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}(\mathrm{R}^{q}f_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{F}^{\prime}_{\lambda})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{O},

(11.11.3)

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}^{q}f_{\mathbf{I}*}(\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{O}^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\mathrm{R}^{q}f_{\mathbf{I}*}(\mathscr{F}^{\prime}_{\lambda}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{O}^{\prime}).

The conclusion follows from
11.10
.
∎

Lemma 11.12
.

With the notation in
11.3
and
11.4
, let
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
be a torsion abelian sheaf on
Y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
,
\mathcal{H}=\mathrm{R}f_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, and we set
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}=\Psi^{\prime}_{*}a^{\prime-1}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
,
\mathscr{H}=\mathrm{R}\Psi_{*}a^{-1}\mathcal{H}
. Let
\widetilde{X}
be an object of
\mathbf{I}_{Y\to X^{Y}}
,
\widetilde{Y}=\overline{\eta}\times_{\overline{S}}\widetilde{X}
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}=g^{\prime-1}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
.

(1)

The sheaf
\mathrm{R}^{q}f_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{F}^{\prime}
is canonically isomorphic to the presheaf
\widetilde{X}\mapsto H^{q}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\widetilde{Y^{\prime}},\widetilde{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}})
for each integer
q
.

(2)

If
Y^{\prime}\to Y
is proper, then there exists a canonical isomorphism
\mathscr{H}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{R}f_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{F}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Note that the canonical morphism
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\to\mathrm{R}\Psi^{\prime}_{*}a^{\prime-1}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is an isomorphism by
3.27
.(
1
). Thus,
\mathrm{R}f_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{F}^{\prime}=\mathrm{R}(\Psi\circ f_{\mathrm{v}})_{*}a^{\prime-1}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, whose
q
-th cohomology is the sheaf associated to the presheaf
\widetilde{X}\mapsto H^{q}_{\mathrm{v}}(\widetilde{Y^{\prime}},\widetilde{a^{\prime}}^{-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}})=H^{q}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\widetilde{Y^{\prime}},\widetilde{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}})
by
3.9
, and thus (1) follows. If
Y^{\prime}\to Y
is proper, then the base change morphism
a^{-1}\mathrm{R}f_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}\to\mathrm{R}f_{\mathrm{v}*}a^{\prime-1}
induces an isomorphism
a^{-1}\mathcal{H}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{R}f_{\mathrm{v}*}a^{\prime-1}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
by
3.10
, and thus (2) follows.
∎

Theorem 11.13
.

With the notation in
11.3
, let
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
be a finite locally constant abelian sheaf on
Y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. Assume that

(i)

the morphism
X^{\prime}\to X
is proper of finite presentation, and that

(ii)

the sheaf
\mathrm{R}^{q}f_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
is finite locally constant for each integer
q
and nonzero for finitely many
q
, and that

(iii)

we have
\mathrm{R}^{q}\psi_{*}\mathbb{H}=0
(resp.
\mathrm{R}^{q}\psi^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{H}=0
) for any finite locally constant abelian sheaf
\mathbb{H}
on
Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
(resp.
Y^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
) and any integer
q>0
.

Then, the relative Faltings’ comparison morphism associated to
f
and
\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
(
10.6.1
) (which exists by
(
iii
)
) is an almost isomorphism in the derived category
\mathbf{D}(\overline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\textrm{-}\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}})

(
5.7
)
, and it induces an almost isomorphism

(11.13.1)

\displaystyle(\psi_{*}\mathrm{R}^{q}f_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}^{q}f_{\mathbf{E}*}(\psi^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\prime})

of
\overline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-modules for each integer
q
.

Proof.

We follow the discussion of
11.6
and set
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}=\Psi^{\prime}_{*}a^{\prime-1}\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
. The canonical morphism (
11.6.8
)

(11.13.2)

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\varepsilon_{*}((\mathrm{R}f_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}\mathscr{O})\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}\varepsilon_{*}\mathrm{R}f_{\mathbf{I}*}(\mathscr{F}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{O}^{\prime})

is an almost isomorphism by
11.7
. It remains to show that the canonical morphism (
11.6.7
)

(11.13.3)

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\varepsilon_{*}(\mathrm{R}f_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}\varepsilon_{*}((\mathrm{R}f_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}\mathscr{O})

is also an almost isomorphism. With the notation in
11.12
by taking
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}=\mathbb{F}^{\prime}
, the complex
\mathcal{H}
is a bounded complex whose cohomologies
H^{q}(\mathcal{H})
are finite locally constant abelian sheaves by condition (
ii
). Consider the commutative diagram (
11.1
),

\textstyle{(\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{/Y})_{\mathrm{v}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a}
\scriptstyle{\Psi}
\textstyle{Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\mathbf{I}_{Y\to X^{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}

We set
\mathcal{L}=\mathrm{R}\psi_{*}\mathcal{H}
. Then,
H^{q}(\mathcal{L})=\psi_{*}H^{q}(\mathcal{H})
by Cartan-Leray spectral sequence and condition (
iii
). Hence,
\mathcal{L}
is a bounded complex of abelian sheaves whose cohomologies are finite locally constant by
10.3
so that the canonical morphism

(11.13.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}\varepsilon_{*}(\varepsilon^{-1}\mathcal{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}\mathscr{O})

is an almost isomorphism by
8.15
.

On the other hand,
H^{q}(\mathscr{H})=\Psi_{*}a^{-1}H^{q}(\mathcal{H})
by Cartan-Leray spectral sequence and
3.27
.(
1
). Thus, the base change morphism
\varepsilon^{-1}\mathrm{R}\psi_{*}\to\mathrm{R}\Psi_{*}a^{-1}
induces an isomorphism
\varepsilon^{-1}\mathcal{L}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{H}
by
11.2
. Moreover, the canonical morphism
\mathcal{L}\to\mathrm{R}\varepsilon_{*}\varepsilon^{-1}\mathcal{L}=\mathrm{R}\varepsilon_{*}\mathscr{H}=\mathrm{R}\psi_{*}\mathrm{R}a_{*}a^{-1}\mathcal{H}
is an isomorphism by
3.9
. Thus, the canonical morphism

(11.13.9)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{R}\varepsilon_{*}\varepsilon^{-1}\mathcal{L})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}\varepsilon_{*}(\varepsilon^{-1}\mathcal{L}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{O})

is an almost isomorphism by (
11.13.8
). In conclusion, (
11.13.3
) is an almost isomorphism by (
11.13.9
) and by the canonical isomorphisms
\varepsilon^{-1}\mathcal{L}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{H}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{R}f_{\mathbf{I}*}\mathscr{F}^{\prime}
.
∎

Remark 11.14
.

We give two concrete situations where the conditions in
11.13
are satisfied:

(1)

Let
\overline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
be the integral closure of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
in an algebraic closure
\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
,
X^{\prime}\to X
a proper and finitely presented morphism of coherent
\overline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-schemes,
Y^{\prime}\to Y
the base change of
X^{\prime}\to X
by
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\overline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})
. Assume that
Y^{\prime}\to Y
is smooth. Then, the condition (
ii
) is guaranteed by
[
SGA 4

III

, XVI.2.2 and XVII.5.2.8.1]
, and the condition (
iii
) is guaranteed by
10.8
.

(2)

Let
\mathcal{O}_{K}
be a strictly Henselian discrete valuation ring with fraction field
K
of characteristic
0
and residue field of characteristic
p
,
\overline{K}
an algebraic closure of
K
,
X^{\prime}\to X
a proper morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-schemes of finite type,
Y^{\prime}\to Y
the base change of
X^{\prime}\to X
by
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\overline{K})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
. Assume that
Y^{\prime}\to Y
is smooth. Then, the condition (
ii
) is guaranteed by
[
SGA 4

III

, XVI.2.2 and XVII.5.2.8.1]
, and the condition (
iii
) is guaranteed by
10.7
.

12.
A Local Version of the Relative Hodge-Tate Filtration

12.1
.

Let
K
be a complete discrete valuation field of characteristic
0
with valuation ring
\mathcal{O}_{K}
whose residue field is algebraically closed (a condition required by
[
AG20
, 4.1.3, 5.1.3]
) of characteristic
p>0
,
\overline{K}
an algebraic closure of
K
,
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{K}}
the integral closure of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
in
\overline{K}
,
\eta=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K)
,
\overline{\eta}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\overline{K})
,
S=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
,
\overline{S}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathcal{O}_{\overline{K}})
. Let
(f,g):(X^{\prime\triangleright}\to X^{\prime})\to(X^{\circ}\to X)
be a morphism of adequate open immersions of schemes (
10.11
) over
\eta\to S
such that
g:X^{\prime}\to X
is projective and that the induced morphism
(X^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{X^{\prime\triangleright}\to X^{\prime}})\to(X,\mathscr{M}_{X^{\circ}\to X})
is smooth and saturated. We are in fact in the situation
10.12
but with a slightly different notation.

Consider the morphisms of sites defined in
7.34
:

\textstyle{X^{\prime\triangleright,\mathbb{N}}_{\overline{\eta},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\breve{f}_{\overline{\eta},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
\textstyle{X^{\circ,\mathbb{N}}_{\overline{\eta},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\breve{\psi}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{X^{\circ}_{\overline{\eta}}\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\breve{\sigma}}
\textstyle{X^{\mathbb{N}}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}.}

We consider
\breve{\sigma}
as a morphism of ringed sites
\breve{\sigma}:(\mathbf{E}_{X^{\circ}_{\overline{\eta}}\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}},\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}})\to(X^{\mathbb{N}}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}})
,
and
\breve{f}_{\overline{\eta},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
as a morphism of ringed sites
\breve{f}_{\overline{\eta},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}:(X^{\prime\triangleright,\mathbb{N}}_{\overline{\eta},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\to(X^{\circ,\mathbb{N}}_{\overline{\eta},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, where
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}=(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})_{n\geq 0}
.

Theorem 12.2

(
[
AG20
, 6.7.5, 6.7.10, 6.7.13]
)
.

Under the assumptions in
12.1
and with the same notation, for each integer
n\geq 0
, there is a canonical
G_{K}
-equivariant finite decreasing filtration
(\mathrm{Fil}^{q})_{q\in\mathbb{Z}}
on the
\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
-module
\breve{\psi}_{*}(\mathrm{R}^{n}\breve{f}_{\overline{\eta},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
(cf.
2.6
) and a canonical
G_{K}
-equivariant isomorphism for each
q\in\mathbb{Z}
,

(12.2.1)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Gr}^{q}(\breve{\psi}_{*}(\mathrm{R}^{n}\breve{f}_{\overline{\eta},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}))\otimes_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}_{\mathbb{Q}})\cong\breve{\sigma}^{*}(\mathrm{R}^{q}g_{*}(\Omega^{n-q}_{(X^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{X^{\prime}})/(X,\mathscr{M}_{X})})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}}\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}(q-n))_{\mathbb{Q}},

where
\mathrm{Gr}^{q}
denotes the graded piece
\mathrm{Fil}^{q}/\mathrm{Fil}^{q+1}
.

We call this filtration the
relative Hodge-Tate filtration
of the morphism
(f,g):(X^{\prime\triangleright}\to X^{\prime})\to(X^{\circ}\to X)
.

Remark 12.3
.

We keep the notation and assumptions in
12.1
and
12.2
.

(1)

If we set
\mathrm{R}^{n}\breve{f}_{\overline{\eta},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\breve{\mathbb{L}}^{(n)}=(\mathbb{L}_{k}^{(n)})_{k\geq 0}
where
\mathbb{L}_{k}^{(n)}=\mathrm{R}^{n}f_{\overline{\eta},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}*}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}\mathbb{Z})
, then
\mathbb{L}_{k}^{(n)}
is a finite locally constant abelian sheaf on
X^{\circ}_{\overline{\eta},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
(
[
AG20
, 2.2.25]
), and the inverse system
(\mathbb{L}_{k}^{(n)})_{k\geq 0}
is Artin-Rees
p
-adic (
[
Fu15
, 10.1.18.(iii)]
).

(2)

The
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{M}^{q,n-q}=\mathrm{R}^{q}g_{*}(\Omega^{n-q}_{(X^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{X^{\prime}})/(X,\mathscr{M}_{X})})
is coherent and its restriction to
X_{\eta}
is locally free (
[
IKN05
, 7.2]
, cf. the proof of
[
AG20
, 6.7.13]
).

12.4
.

Under the assumptions in
12.1
,
12.2
,
12.3
and with the same notation, assume further that
X=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)
is affine. We remark that
R
is
p
-torsion free (
[
AGT16
, II.6.3.(ii)]
).
Let
V\to U
be an object of
\mathbf{E}_{X^{\circ}_{\overline{\eta}}\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
satisfying the following conditions:

(1)

The morphism
V\to U
is Faltings acyclic (cf.
8.1
,
8.24
).

(2)

For any integers
n\geq 0
and
k\geq 0
, the pullback
\mathbb{L}_{k}^{(n)}|_{V_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
is constant with value
H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},k}
.

We denote by
A
the
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra
\overline{\mathscr{B}}(V\to U)
(i.e.
U^{V}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)
), and we set
H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}=\lim_{k\to\infty}H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},k}
.

Remark 12.5
.

Let
\overline{x}
be a geometric point of
V
. Then, there is a natural isomorphism
H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},k}\cong H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X^{\prime\triangleright}_{\overline{x}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}\mathbb{Z})
(
[
AG20
, 2.2.25]
). We remark that
H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X^{\prime\triangleright}_{\overline{x}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})=\lim_{k\to\infty}H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X^{\prime\triangleright}_{\overline{x}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}\mathbb{Z})
is a finitely generated
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module (thus so is
H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
), and that the morphism of inverse systems of abelian groups

(12.5.1)

\displaystyle(H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X^{\prime\triangleright}_{\overline{x}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})/p^{k}H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X^{\prime\triangleright}_{\overline{x}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}))_{k\geq 0}\longrightarrow(H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},k})_{k\geq 0}

is an Artin-Rees isomorphism, by which we mean its kernel and cokernel are Artin-Rees zero (cf.
12.3
.(
1
) and
[
Fu15
, 10.1.4]
).

12.6
.

Following
12.4
, notice that
U^{\circ}=X^{\circ}\times_{X}U
is affine and geometrically normal over
K
and that
V
is affine and normal, since
X^{\circ}
is smooth over
K
. We assume further that the following conditions hold:

(3)

The scheme
V
is integral and lies over a connected component
U^{\circ}_{\star}
of
U^{\circ}
.

(4)

The function field
\mathcal{L}
of
V
is a Galois extension of the function field
\mathcal{K}
of
U^{\circ}_{\star}
with Galois group
\Gamma
.

Let
U^{\circ}_{\overline{\eta},\star}
be the connected component of
U^{\circ}_{\overline{\eta}}
over which
V
lies. Its function field is the composite
\overline{K}\mathcal{K}
of
\overline{K}
and
\mathcal{K}
in
\mathcal{L}
, which is Galois over
\mathcal{K}
whose Galois group identifies with the closed subgroup
G_{L}
of
G_{K}
where
L
is the algebraic closure of
K
in
\mathcal{K}
. We denote by
\Delta
the Galois group of
\mathcal{L}
over
\overline{K}\mathcal{K}
. It is clear that
G_{L}=\Gamma/\Delta
.

\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma}
\textstyle{U^{\circ}_{\overline{\eta},\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{G_{L}}
\textstyle{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\overline{K})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{G_{K}}
\textstyle{U^{\circ}_{\star}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(K)}

Since
V
is the integral closure of
U^{\circ}_{\star}
in
\mathcal{L}
, the canonical homomorphism of groups

(12.6.7)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Aut}_{U^{\circ}_{\star}}(V)^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Aut}_{\mathcal{K}}(\mathcal{L})=\Gamma

is an isomorphism. In particular,
\Gamma
acts naturally on
V
on the right. For
\gamma\in\Gamma
with image
u\in G_{K}
, we denote by
f_{\gamma}:V\to V
the right action of
\gamma
on
V
, and for any
K
-scheme
Z
, we denote by
f_{u}:Z_{\overline{\eta}}\to Z_{\overline{\eta}}
the base change of the automorphism of
\overline{\eta}
induced by
u
. There is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{\gamma}}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U^{\circ}_{\overline{\eta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{u}}
\textstyle{U^{\circ}_{\overline{\eta}}}

The natural isomorphism (induced by the base change)

(12.6.12)

\displaystyle f_{\gamma,\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}(\mathbb{L}_{k}^{(n)}|_{V_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{L}_{k}^{(n)}|_{V_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}

defines a natural action of
\Gamma
on
H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},k}
, and thus an action on
H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}=\lim_{k}H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},k}
. On the other hand,
\Gamma
also acts naturally on
A
as
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)
is the integral closure of
U
in
\mathcal{L}
, and thus acts on the Tate twist
A(i)
via the map
\Gamma\to G_{K}
.

Theorem 12.7
.

Under the assumptions in
12.4
and with the same notation, for each integer
n\geq 0
, there is a canonical finite decreasing filtration
(\mathrm{fil}^{q})_{q\in\mathbb{Z}}
on
H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widehat{A}[1/p]
and a canonical isomorphism for each
q\in\mathbb{Z}
,

(12.7.1)

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}^{q}(H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widehat{A}[\frac{1}{p}])\cong H^{q}(X^{\prime},\Omega^{n-q}_{(X^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{X^{\prime}})/(X,\mathscr{M}_{X})})\otimes_{R}\widehat{A}[\frac{1}{p}](q-n),

where
\mathrm{gr}^{q}
denotes the graded piece
\mathrm{fil}^{q}/\mathrm{fil}^{q+1}
. Moreover, under the assumptions in
12.6
and with the same notation, the filtration
(\mathrm{fil}^{q})_{q\in\mathbb{Z}}
and the isomorphisms (
12.7.1
) are
\Gamma
-equivariant.

Proof.

We set
Y=X^{\circ}_{\overline{\eta}}
. We start from the filtration of
12.2
. Consider the natural morphism of ringed sites (
7.34.2
)
\breve{\nu}:(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}},\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}})\to(\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}},\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}})
. We obtain a filtration
\breve{\nu}^{*}\mathrm{Fil}^{q}
on
\breve{\nu}^{*}(\breve{\psi}_{*}\breve{\mathbb{L}}^{(n)}\otimes_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}_{\mathbb{Q}})
with graded pieces
\breve{\nu}^{*}\mathrm{Gr}^{q}=\breve{\nu}^{*}\breve{\sigma}^{*}\breve{\mathcal{M}}^{q,n-q}(q-n)_{\mathbb{Q}}
(as
\breve{\nu}^{*}=\breve{\nu}^{-1}
is exact, cf. (
7.34.3
)). We apply the derived functor
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{V\to U}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}},-)
to these modules.

Consider the commutative diagram

\textstyle{V_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{V\to U}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{V\to U}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{Y_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{Y\to X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu}

Since the canonical morphism
j^{-1}\psi_{*}\mathbb{L}^{(n)}_{k}\to\psi_{*}j^{-1}\mathbb{L}^{(n)}_{k}
is an isomorphism by
10.9
,
j^{-1}\psi_{*}\mathbb{L}^{(n)}_{k}
is constant with value
H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},k}
by
10.3
. Thus, the restriction of
\breve{\nu}^{-1}\breve{\psi}_{*}\breve{\mathbb{L}}^{(n)}
to
\mathbf{E}_{V\to U}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}
is the inverse system of constant sheaves
(H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},k})_{k\geq 0}
. Therefore, the canonical morphism

(12.7.6)

\displaystyle H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},k}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}A\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{V\to U}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\nu^{*}(\psi_{*}\mathbb{L}_{k}^{(n)}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}))

is an almost isomorphism, since
V\to U
is Faltings acyclic and
H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},k}
is a finite abelian group. Notice that for any integer
q\geq 0
there exists a canonical exact sequence (
[
AGT16
, VI.7.10]
)

(12.7.7)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathrm{R}^{1}\lim_{k\to\infty}H^{q-1}(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}_{V\to U},\nu^{*}(\psi_{*}\mathbb{L}_{k}^{(n)}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}))\to

\displaystyle H^{q}(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{V\to U},\breve{\nu}^{*}(\breve{\psi}_{*}\breve{\mathbb{L}}^{(n)}\otimes_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}))

\displaystyle\to\lim_{k\to\infty}H^{q}(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}_{V\to U},\nu^{*}(\psi_{*}\mathbb{L}_{k}^{(n)}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}))\to 0.

Since the inverse system
(H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},k}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}A)_{k\geq 0}
is Artin-Rees
p
-adic,
\mathrm{R}^{1}\lim_{k\to\infty}H^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}_{V\to U},\nu^{*}(\psi_{*}\mathbb{L}_{k}^{(n)}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}))
is almost zero by the almost isomorphism (
12.7.6
). Moreover, we deduce that
\lim_{k\to\infty}(H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},k}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}A)\to H^{0}(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{V\to U},\breve{\nu}^{*}(\breve{\psi}_{*}\breve{\mathbb{L}}^{(n)}\otimes_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}))
is an almost isomorphism and that
H^{q}(\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{V\to U},\breve{\nu}^{*}(\breve{\psi}_{*}\breve{\mathbb{L}}^{(n)}\otimes_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}))
is almost zero for
q>0
. Since
H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is a finitely generated
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module (
12.5
), we have
\lim_{k\to\infty}(H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},k}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}A)=H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widehat{A}
. Therefore, the canonical morphism

(12.7.8)

\displaystyle H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widehat{A}\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{V\to U}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}},\breve{\nu}^{*}(\breve{\psi}_{*}\breve{\mathbb{L}}^{(n)}\otimes_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}))

is an almost isomorphism. Inverting
p
, we obtain a canonical isomorphism

(12.7.9)

\displaystyle H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widehat{A}[\frac{1}{p}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{V\to U}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}},\breve{\nu}^{*}(\breve{\psi}_{*}\breve{\mathbb{L}}^{(n)}\otimes_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}))[\frac{1}{p}].

By taking
H^{0}(\mathbf{E}_{V\to U}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}},\breve{\nu}^{*}\mathrm{Fil}^{q})
, the filtration
\breve{\nu}^{*}\mathrm{Fil}^{q}
on
\breve{\nu}^{*}(\breve{\psi}_{*}\breve{\mathbb{L}}^{(n)}\otimes_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}_{\mathbb{Q}})
induces a canonical filtration
\mathrm{fil}^{q}
on
H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X^{\prime\triangleright}_{\overline{x}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widehat{A}[1/p]
(cf.
2.6
).

On the other hand, recall that
\mathcal{M}^{q,n-q}
is the coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module associated to the coherent
R
-module
M^{q,n-q}=H^{q}(X^{\prime},\Omega^{n-q}_{(X^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{X^{\prime}})/(X,\mathscr{M}_{X})})
and that
M^{q,n-q}[1/p]
is a projective
R[1/p]
-module (
12.3
.(
2
)). By
8.4
, we see that the canonical morphism

(12.7.10)

\displaystyle H^{q}(X^{\prime},\Omega^{n-q}_{(X^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{X^{\prime}})/(X,\mathscr{M}_{X})})\otimes_{R}\widehat{A}[\frac{1}{p}]\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{V\to U}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}},\breve{\nu}^{*}\breve{\sigma}^{*}\breve{\mathcal{M}}^{q,n-q})[\frac{1}{p}]

is an isomorphism. Thus, the canonical isomorphisms for the graded pieces
\breve{\nu}^{*}\mathrm{Gr}^{q}=\breve{\nu}^{*}\breve{\sigma}^{*}\breve{\mathcal{M}}^{q,n-q}(q-n)_{\mathbb{Q}}
induce canonical isomorphisms (
12.7.1
). This completes the proof of the first statement.

For the
\Gamma
-equivariance, let
\gamma\in\Gamma
with image
u\in G_{K}
. We obtain from
12.6
a commutative diagram

\textstyle{(V\to U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f_{\gamma},\mathrm{id}_{U})}
\textstyle{(V\to U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(Y\to X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f_{u},\mathrm{id}_{X})}
\textstyle{(Y\to X)}

where the vertical arrows are the same pro-étale morphism. It induces a commutative diagram of fibred ringed sites over
\mathbb{N}
,

\textstyle{\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{V\to U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{\gamma}}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{V\to U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{Y\to X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{u}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{Y\to X}}

where the vertical morphisms induce the same localization morphism of the associated topoi (
[
AGT16
, III.7.9]
). Recall that the
G_{K}
-actions on the
\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}
-modules
\breve{\nu}^{*}(\breve{\psi}_{*}\breve{\mathbb{L}}^{(n)}\otimes_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}})
,
\breve{\nu}^{*}\breve{\sigma}^{*}\breve{\mathcal{M}}^{q,n-q}(q-n)
on
\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}}_{Y\to X}
define isomorphisms

(12.7.19)

\displaystyle\breve{\nu}^{*}(\breve{\psi}_{*}\breve{\mathbb{L}}^{(n)}\otimes_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}f_{u}^{-1}(\breve{\nu}^{*}(\breve{\psi}_{*}\breve{\mathbb{L}}^{(n)}\otimes_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}})),

(12.7.20)

\displaystyle\breve{\nu}^{*}\breve{\sigma}^{*}\breve{\mathcal{M}}^{q,n-q}(q-n)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}f_{u}^{-1}(\breve{\nu}^{*}\breve{\sigma}^{*}\breve{\mathcal{M}}^{q,n-q}(q-n)).

By
12.2
, up to isogenies, they are respectively compatible with the relative Hodge-Tate filtration
\breve{\nu}^{*}\mathrm{Fil}^{q}
and compatible with the canonical isomorphisms for the graded pieces
\breve{\nu}^{*}\mathrm{Gr}^{q}
. Passing to localization by
j^{-1}
, we obtain from the isomorphisms (
12.7.19
) and (
12.7.20
) the isomorphisms

(12.7.21)

\displaystyle j^{-1}(\breve{\nu}^{*}(\breve{\psi}_{*}\breve{\mathbb{L}}^{(n)}\otimes_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}j^{-1}f_{u}^{-1}(\breve{\nu}^{*}(\breve{\psi}_{*}\breve{\mathbb{L}}^{(n)}\otimes_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}f_{\gamma}^{-1}j^{-1}(\breve{\nu}^{*}(\breve{\psi}_{*}\breve{\mathbb{L}}^{(n)}\otimes_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\breve{\overline{\mathscr{B}}})),

(12.7.22)

\displaystyle j^{-1}(\breve{\nu}^{*}\breve{\sigma}^{*}\breve{\mathcal{M}}^{q,n-q}(q-n))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}j^{-1}f_{u}^{-1}(\breve{\nu}^{*}\breve{\sigma}^{*}\breve{\mathcal{M}}^{q,n-q}(q-n))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}f_{\gamma}^{-1}j^{-1}(\breve{\nu}^{*}\breve{\sigma}^{*}\breve{\mathcal{M}}^{q,n-q}(q-n)).

Applying the derived functor
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbf{E}_{V\to U}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\mathbb{N}},-)[1/p]
and combining with the canonical isomorphisms (
12.7.9
) and (
12.7.10
), we obtain automorphisms of
H^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widehat{A}[1/p]
and of
H^{q}(X^{\prime},\Omega^{n-q}_{(X^{\prime},\mathscr{M}_{X^{\prime}})/(X,\mathscr{M}_{X})})\otimes_{R}\widehat{A}[1/p](q-n)
, which are exactly the semi-linear actions of
\gamma\in\Gamma
on these
\widehat{A}[1/p]
-modules defined in
12.6
by going through the definitions. Thus, we see that the actions of
\Gamma
are compatible with the relative Hodge-Tate filtration and the canonical isomorphisms for the graded pieces, which completes the proof.
∎

Remark 12.8
.

The arguments for
12.7
does not make use of the assumption that the residue field of
K
is algebraically closed.

Remark 12.9
.

In
12.6
, we take
U
to be an étale neighborhood of a point of the special fibre of
X
which is affine and admits an adequate chart in the sense of
[
AGT16
, III.4.4]
(cf.
[
AGT16
, III.4.7]
), and take
V
to be the inverse limit of the normalized universal cover
(V_{i})
of
U^{\circ}_{\star}
at
\overline{x}
(cf.
[
SGA 1
, V.7]
,
[
AGT16
, VI.9.8]
). We set
U^{V_{i}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(R_{i})
and
\overline{R}=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}(R_{i})
. Then,
\Gamma=\pi_{1}(U^{\circ}_{\star},\overline{x})
and
A=\overline{R}
. We obtain from the adequate chart finitely many nonzero divisors
f_{1},\dots,f_{r}
of
\Gamma(U_{\eta},\mathcal{O}_{U_{\eta}})
such that the divisor
D=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{div}(f_{i})
has support
U_{\eta}\setminus U^{\circ}
and that at each strict henselization of
U_{\eta}
those elements
f_{i}
contained in the maximal ideal form a subset of a regular system of parameters (cf.
[
AG20
, 4.2.2.(ii)]
). Then,
A
is almost pre-perfectoid and admits compatible
n
-th power roots of
f_{i}
(
[
AGT16
, II.9.10]
). Hence,
V\to U
is Faltings acyclic by
8.24
, and thus Theorem
12.7
holds in this setting, which gives a local version of the relative Hodge-Tate filtration answering the question of Abbes-Gros raised in the first version of
[
AG20
]
(cf.
[
AG20
, 1.2.3]
).
